Checked: the instance's 8 new tests (who may, the name rule, the picture's lifecycle and blob, retention, the fan-out to members and to nobody else, the permissions in the snapshot); a live core test that another member is told; on the desktop 7 tests and a live one where a second account's shell, told through its account log, shows the new name and fetches the picture; a browser-suite step in Chromium and WebKit; on iOS 9 kit tests and `scripts/smoke-server-settings.sh`, new; on Android 8 unit tests and a section of `smoke.sh` (rename typed, retention unchanged until Save, the picture's pixel read off the rail). **Found and fixed on the way**: the instance looked a permission's wire name up as an existing atom, which fails before the generated enum module has loaded; the desktop drew a person's or a server's initials under a decoded picture, which a picture with transparency shows through. **Not verified**: choosing a picture through either phone's photo picker (the phones' smokes set it with the CLI); the refusals worded on the phones met live; the daily purge after a shortened retention (its job is the one tested since step 2). **Found and left**: neither phone draws people's pictures (avatars are initials there; Android's new `LumiaPicture` and iOS's `LumiaPictureOver` could carry them) and the iOS Profile has no photo, which the plan's Profile item already covers; on Android in the dark theme a disabled primary button looks almost enabled. Roles and permissions themselves still have no screen on any client, so `MANAGE_SERVER` can be granted only by the API.
- [x] Autoscroll with the middle button, and no focus ring on the web's fields (2026-11-13, from Ryan: "can we add scroll with middle mouse held down and dragged?", autoscroll chosen over grab-and-drag, "it needs to work on web too", and "on web, I don't want the focused input ring to show up at all"). Held over any pane that scrolls and moved up or down from where it came down, the middle button scrolls the pane continuously, nothing within 8 points of the press, then 8 points a second for each point past that, at most 4,000, until it comes up; a ring with a dot marks the press. **Desktop**: every pane through `ui/scroll.rs`; Rune gives an element with a drag handler the primary button's presses and turns off selecting text in it, and takes no pull requests, so the handler is on the scroll bar's frame and the pane has a hover handler that does nothing, which stops a primary press at the pane: the 12 selection tests pass as before. **Web**: one listener for the page (`client/autoscroll.ts`), on pointer events (with the middle button down a browser sends no mouse moves once the press is cancelled), cancelling the browser's own autoscroll and Linux's middle-click paste over a pane. A text field and the composer draw no focus ring; their caret and border show it; buttons and links keep it. Checked: a desktop test (dead zone, up while held, on while held, stops on release, a primary drag starts nothing, the mark); a browser-suite step in Chromium and WebKit. **Not verified**: a real mouse in a window on any desktop, or in a browser; macOS trackpads have no middle button, so on a Mac it needs a mouse.
- [x] The member list's toggle, and a slideover in a narrow window (2026-11-13, from Ryan: "add a member list toggle" and "hide member list at certain widths, and make the member list a slideover"; the desktop shell and the web, the same way). The conversation's header has a toggle ("Hide members" and "Show members" in a server, "details" in a personal conversation), pressed while the aside shows. A window 1100 points or wider has the aside in the row and the toggle puts it away and brings it back, remembered on this computer or browser (`settings.aside_hidden`, the web's preferences). A narrower window has no room for it: it is out of the row, and the toggle slides it over the conversation's end from the window's edge, over a shade; a press on the shade, or Escape, slides it away, and a window widened past the breakpoint takes it back into the row. Before, the desktop always had it in the row with no toggle, and the web had a toggle only when narrow, which popped the aside in. Checked: two desktop tests (the toggle and its memory in a wide window; the slideover, its place at the window's end, the shade, Escape and widening in a narrow one) and a browser-suite step in Chromium and WebKit at 1440 and 900 wide, with headless pictures of both. **Not verified**: resizing a real window across the breakpoint by hand.
- [x] Display settings (2026-11-13, from Ryan: "we need to add some display settings. padding, display avatars, display embeds, display images, font size, etc. and instead of just comfortable and compact, we will add minimal -- and these will just be presets for these settings, that users can tweak however they like"; settled in a grill the same day, Minimal as a one-line layout by Ryan's choice). On all four clients, the device's and never the account's, as Appearance is. **The settings**, with their value names on every client: **Layout**, `grouped` (the blocks as drawn today) or `one_line`; **Spacing**, `roomy` (today's comfortable: edge, block gap and divider 8, beside 14, under the name 4), `cozy` (today's compact: 4, 4, 4, beside 10, under the name 2) or `tight` (cozy with the edge and the block gap 2); **Avatars**, `large`, `small` or `hidden`, now independent of spacing; **Font size**, 13, 14, 15 (the default), 16, 18 or 20, the message text's size, every text in a message row (name, times, the reply line, mention pills, reactions, "(edited)", system lines, the day divider) and the composer's text scaled by the same ratio over 15, line heights with them, avatars and room not, and on the phones on top of the system's text scale; **Images** and **Embeds**, `shown` or `link`, in the model and the presets only, with no control until attachments and unfurling exist (a control that does nothing is worse than none). **Reactions** (2026-11-14, #10), `shown` or `hidden`, with a control: Minimal hides them. Stored as `layout`, `spacing`, `avatars`, `font_size` (`fontSize` on the web), `images`, `embeds`, each read as its default when missing or not understood; `density` goes, with no migration. **The presets** write their values and are not stored; font size is never part of one. Comfortable (the default): grouped, roomy, large, shown, shown. Compact: grouped, cozy, small, shown, shown. Minimal: one line, tight, hidden, link, link. The page shows the preset whose values all match (avatars not compared in one line), or none selected and "Custom". **One line**: `12:04  Priya  message text` on every line, the time and the name repeated even within one person's run; the time always shown in `text-meta`, as the system says 12 or 24 hours, the name in `text-author` and not coloured; a message that runs on wraps under the start of its text; no avatar in any case. Spacing there is the room between lines, 4, 2 or 0, the divider's as each step has it. A reply's reference is a short line over the message at the text's start (`↳ Priya: first words…`), its reactions a row under it at the same start; "(edited)", the mention tint, the "New" line, a jump's flash, the toolbar, selecting and copying (as grouped copies), a join line (the time, then the line) and the row as one accessibility stop all as in grouped. **Avatars hidden in grouped**: no gutter left, the name starting where the avatar did, and the name opens the profile card (as the avatar does). **The page**: Preferences' Messages card has the preset as a segmented choice (Comfortable, Compact, Minimal), then Layout (Grouped, One line), Spacing (Roomy, Cozy, Tight), Avatars (Large, Small, Hidden; not drawn in one line) and Font size (13 to 20). No canvas board first: the design system's pieces, as the Preferences page was. Debug builds take `LUMIA_DEV_PRESET` and `LUMIA_DEV_FONT_SIZE` for a launch, in place of `LUMIA_DEV_DENSITY`; each client's smoke draws Minimal, and how one line reads on a phone is looked at in those pictures. The web in the main checkout; the desktop, iOS and Android by an agent each in a worktree. **Built** on all four the same day, with what each had to decide: in grouped with avatars hidden a block's later messages draw no time (there is no column for it), their accessibility label still saying who and when; the desktop, iOS and Android give one line's time a column as wide as the widest time so the names start together, and the phones cut a long name short (iOS past 45% of the width, Android past a quarter) so the words keep the rest, where the web lets each line's time and name take what they need; the desktop places a reply's reference at the text's start with a spacer of the reference's height in the time-and-name column, copies one line as grouped copies (the name and time as the first text's prefix), hangs the toolbar over the row above in one line so it does not cover the words, and reads the system's 12 or 24 hours through `lumia-sparkle` (`clock_24_hour`); Android's times now follow the phone's 24-hour switch, which they had ignored, and its gap beside the avatar is the spec's 14 and 10, so Comfortable there moved 2 points from what it drew; the web's font size is `--msg-scale` on the root. The desktop had no `LUMIA_DEV_DENSITY`, so it has no launch names either; its scenes (`blocks-minimal`, `blocks-font-20`, `blocks-avatars-hidden`, `replies-minimal`, `preferences-custom`) do that job. **Checked**: the web's first unit tests (`bun run test`, 7, now in `web.yml`) and the browser suite in Chromium and WebKit, whose Preferences step chooses Compact, Minimal, 20, a Custom between and checks the lines after a reload; the desktop's 220 tests (7 new unit, 6 new in `tests/preferences.rs`, one copy test) and `scripts/smoke.sh` against a scratch instance, its timeline bench (grouped unchanged within noise; one line 0.264 ms p50 a frame scrolling against 0.189, from mounting 85 rows to 32, each about 0.55 of a grouped one); iOS 79 kit tests (18 of Preferences) and `smoke-ios`, `smoke-preferences` and `smoke-typing` in an iOS 17.4 simulator; Android 67 kit tests (12 of Preferences) and the whole `smoke.sh` on an API 35 emulator. Comfortable draws pixel for pixel as before on the web's boards and the desktop's `replies`, `bodies` and `channels` scenes; on the phones it was judged by eye. **Fixed on the way**: the desktop reset the whole `settings.json` (the instance with it) for one value it did not understand, and now defaults each key alone; Android's reaction chips had a fixed height that larger text would clip; Android's smoke checked the header's colour for the server picture rather than the rail's tile. **Not verified**: no real window, phone or browser by hand, so nobody has typed into the page or watched a choice take effect live; nobody has listened to VoiceOver or TalkBack; the font size on top of a raised system text size on either phone; a personal conversation in one line or with avatars hidden on the phones; editing and a reply's jump in one line on the phones; the desktop's 24-hour clock against System Settings; no release build of either phone; Windows and Linux not built; the desktop's smoke drew the default layout only. **Seen and left**: `↳` draws small in the fallback font on the desktop and Android; at Tight a reply's reference sits close to the message above it; at 20 on a phone names past about six characters are cut. **Found, not this work's**: iOS `smoke-replies` fails at its edit step on master before this work too (on iOS 17.4 `app.textFields["message-editor"]` finds nothing; on iOS 27 Return in the editor adds a line rather than saving); on the desktop, the toolbar over the message being replied to covers the lower half of the "New" label in grouped as well.

Android: gateway events can be dropped in a burst (`LumiaClient.start` sends into a 64-slot buffer with `trySend`); the connection line under Home prints the core's reason as it comes (a `CloseFrame { … }` and all); a loop that ended for a reason other than the session is asked about once and then stays ended until a relaunch, where the desktop keeps asking. iOS: CI does not set `LUMIA_TEST_INSTANCE`, so the session test is skipped there. Both: the Profile pages have neither the Account card nor the photo (step 9), and no client wipes a revoked session's store (protocol 6.8).

Requesting it is yours, and Ryan said to (2026-11-12); the form is Apple's, under his developer account, so it waits on him. Once granted, the extension's entitlements and its profile carry it and the extension's `.nothing` (already built, `LumiaClient.notice`) starts hiding the notice. **CI**: `apps/apple/scripts/smoke-signout.sh` and `apps/android/scripts/smoke-signout.sh` are not in a workflow, and the changes to `smoke-push.sh`, which the macOS workflow runs, have not run on a runner; adding runs costs minutes. **The boards** still draw the old sign-out arrow. Also seen and left: the iOS sidebar names the host `localhost_4031` where sign-in says `localhost`.

- [ ] **Who is typing in a personal conversation** (asked for by Ryan, 2026-11-12; not started). What is specified today cannot be built as it stands: protocol 6.7 keeps typing for `PERSONAL_*` channels off the gateway in the clear, and the E2EE spec (section 5, `DmTyping`) puts it in an MLS application message, which is an event appended to the conversation's log for good and a push with generic text to every sleeping device of the other side, once for every five seconds someone types. Section 13 of that spec lists it open. The design to write down and then build, in this order:
  1. *The spec first* (`docs/specs/e2ee.md` sections 5 and 13, protocol 6.7): typing in a personal conversation is a frame the instance relays to the conversation's other subscribed sessions and never stores, never replays and never pushes. Its body is sealed with the suite's AEAD under a key taken from the group's exporter for the current epoch (a label of its own, the channel id as context), and carries the sender's leaf and `until`; a device that cannot open it (another epoch, not a member) drops it without a word. A member could seal one in another member's name, which a signature by the leaf's key would close at the price of a signature every five seconds: decide which, and say so. **What the instance learns must be written into the spec's trust statement and is the part that is Ryan's to accept**: it relays the frame, so it sees which account is typing in which conversation and when, where today it sees only who sent a stored message, when and how large. It never sees content. The alternatives are no typing in personal conversations, or a setting that turns one's own off. Also to settle there: whether a group conversation above some size sends it at all (the open item as written).
  2. *Instance*: `Typing` for a `PERSONAL_*` channel with a sealed body is relayed to the participants' other subscribed sessions under the same spacing rule as a server's channel; without a body it stays dropped, as now. Tests beside the existing five.
  3. *Core*: the DM engine seals on `App::typing` for a personal conversation (the same call every client already makes on an edit) and opens what arrives, reporting the `AppEvent::Typing` the clients already draw. A scenario for the epoch changing under someone typing.
  4. *Clients*: the composer of a personal conversation calls `typing` (each client holds it back today) and draws the line it already has for a channel. The iOS Conversation board already draws it there.
- [ ] What the boards still draw and the shell does not, each for want of the feature behind it: unread dots and mention counts (no read state in the protocol); search of messages, and the sidebar's search field with it; the Threads, Mentions and Saved tiles and the Thread tab; reactions; attachments; the message action toolbar; role tags; formatting, emoji and mention in the composer; the header's call, pins, search and settings; private channels and personal conversations listed in a server's sidebar; on sign-in, "Keep me signed in", "Forgot password?" and the footer links; on the card, a team, a local time and a call; in the account menu, Preferences and Keyboard shortcuts. These are product decisions, not drawing: which of them comes first is Ryan's to say.
- [x] Budgets on the shell itself, release build, this Mac, one instance: launch to the last channel's first frame 288 ms at the median of ten (901 ms on the first run after a build) against 1 s; idle a minute after connecting 119 MB resident (81 MB footprint) against 150 MB. `scripts/startup.py` times a launch.
- [x] Which kind of conversation this is, at a glance (2026-11-15, from Ryan on the desktop shell: "make this more noticeable (green?)", "also maybe make this a warning colour?", "keep both pinned to the top", "add the server name"; every client, the phones by two agents each in a worktree). A personal conversation's encrypted notice is in the green tone and a server DM's note in the amber one, with the eye in place of the shield (a shield reads as safe); the other notices stay as they were. Both stand under the header and stay there as the messages scroll: the desktop shell had them so already; the web's encrypted notice and both phones' were the first rows of the scrolling list and went with it. A server DM's header names its server where a personal conversation's has its detail (desktop and web; the phones had it as the subtitle already). Desktop 29a6d58 (`tokens::secure`, `components::toned_notice`), web efda96e (`Notice` tone `secure`), iOS 489b2b8 (`NoticeTone.secure`), Android 7c86cf9 (`NoticeTone.SECURE` and `WARNING`). Seen: the desktop `server-dm` and `home-dm` scenes and the web gallery in light and dark; the server DM on the iOS simulator and the Android emulator, each against a scratch instance, scrolled under the pinned amber note. Not seen: a personal conversation full of messages scrolling under the green note on either phone (neither test phone got into an encrypted group), any of it on a device, the web's browser suite.
- [ ] Idle memory at a large window (2026-11-15, the released app on this Mac after 8.5 hours, idle): 186 MB resident and 227 MB footprint (peak 444 MB), against the 150 MB budget and the 81 MB footprint above. 110 MB of it is three 36.6 MB IOSurfaces, the window's triple-buffered drawable, which scales with window area and pixel depth (36.6 MB is about 4.8 Mpx at 8 bytes a pixel, a wide-gamut half-float format, or 9.6 Mpx at 4); malloc holds about 66 MB. Not a leak as far as this shows; not yet looked into: whether the drawable needs half floats or three buffers, and what raised the peak. For comparison, Discord on the same Mac at the same time: about 1.09 GB across six processes (its renderer 625 MB) and about 13 times the CPU time per hour.
- [x] CI: `shell.yml`, a Linux job (format, clippy, tests, one frame drawn by Mesa's software Vulkan driver). Every step was run first in ubuntu:24.04 containers: all on arm64; all but the drawing on emulated x86-64, where the driver offered no 16-bit floats, so the drawing step may fail without failing the job until a real runner shows it passing.
- [x] Linux and Windows as far as a container goes: on Linux (arm64) the shell builds, passes its tests and draws signed in to the dev instance; for Windows (`x86_64-pc-windows-gnu`) it type-checks. No window on a screen on either.
- [x] The macOS updater (2026-11-11; architecture 13, "On macOS, as built"). `crates/sparkle` is a bridge crate, the one place in the shell where `unsafe` is allowed (nine blocks, one message send or the load each, with the reason beside it); it loads Sparkle 2.10.1 from the bundle at run time, so the binary links nothing of it. `scripts/bundle-macos.sh` fetches Sparkle pinned by SHA-256, embeds and signs it inside out; the `Info.plist` names the feed and the key and requires the feed itself to be signed. The application menu has "Check for updates…" and "Check for updates automatically". `scripts/dist-macos.sh` makes a release: the disk image, the signed appcast, a delta from the previous image, and Developer ID signing with notarization when the credentials are there. `scripts/update-check.sh` proves it with no hands on a window, under a scratch identifier with throwaway keys and a feed on localhost: an installed build updated itself from the full image and from a delta, and refused a release signed with another key, an image signed with another key, an image altered after signing, an older feed relabelled as newer, and a validly signed feed offering an older build as a newer one. Nobody has seen Sparkle's windows or the menu items.

Found on the way and fixed, each with a test:
- [x] Instance: revoking a device did not end a session made by creating an account with a password, because that request carried no device id and the instance minted a random one for the session. `CreateAccountRequest.device_id` (field 7); the core and the CLI send it.
- [x] Instance: a server's channels were ordered by `position` alone and came back in heap order; ties break by id.
- [x] Core: a device's label was stored in the roster and never read back, so every shell named devices by kind.
- [x] Core: a device that gained its identity while the gateway was connected published no key packages and could not be added to a group until its next connection (`announce_device`).
- [x] Core: `App::dm_send` returned nothing, so a send could not be tied to its message or to its refusal; it returns the message id.
- [x] Shell: a server's member list did not gain someone who joined until a relaunch.
- [x] Core: `App::members` read the first hundred members of a server and no more; it reads every page (checked against a server of 216 on the dev instance, which the shell then listed in full).
- [x] Core: a personal-conversation call made while an identity call held the identity's stores failed with "an identity flow is running", because the engine is built on first use with a `try_lock`. The async calls wait for the stores.
- [x] Shell: the Intel half of a universal build stopped at launch. rustc's default deployment target for Intel is macOS 10.12, and for a system that old the linker names `@rpath/libswiftCoreMedia.dylib`, which the binary has no run path to find. `.cargo/config.toml` sets the target to 14.0, the bundle's stated minimum, and `scripts/bundle-macos.sh` refuses a binary that links anything by a run path. Found by running the Intel slice under Rosetta, which is as far as Intel has been checked: it draws the sign-in screen there; no Intel Mac has run it.

Removed (2026-11-11, architecture 13 "Transition"):
- [x] The Tauri shell: `apps/desktop/src-tauri` with its `package.json`, the `desktop.yml` workflow and the release workflow's Windows and Linux job (with `latest.json` and the minisign key, which no build reads any more; the `TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY` secret can be deleted from the repository's settings). With it went what existed only for it: the `tauri://localhost` origins in the instance's CORS and gateway origin rule (`LumiaWeb.Plugs.Cors` trusts the instance's own origin and the configured web origins, nothing else), the web UI's `inShell()` and its branches (link wording, the address field on an instance-served build, no service worker), and `lumia-wasm`'s `shell` flag that made a device NATIVE from a web build. A web build is always a WEB device now. Checked: the instance's CORS tests, the core's wasm32 clippy, the web typecheck and build, and the browser suite in Chromium and WebKit against the dev instance. A release tag now builds macOS, iOS and Android only.
- [x] The SwiftUI macOS app: `apps/apple/Lumia-macOS` with `scripts/dist.sh` and `scripts/smoke.sh`, and its build and smoke in `macos.yml`. The shell took what it had on the Mac: the bundle identifier `chat.lumia`, the `lumia:` scheme, the `appcast.xml` feed and its Ed25519 key, and the release workflow's macOS job. The shell's data directory is `chat.lumia` under the platform's application-data directory (it was `chat.lumia.desktop` while two apps existed); nothing is moved, so a copy signed in under the old name starts signed out unless the directory is renamed by hand. `apps/apple` is LumiaKit and the iOS app; LumiaKit still builds for macOS because that is where `swift test` runs. Checked: LumiaKit's tests; the iOS app was not rebuilt (comments only changed there).
- [x] CI after the removals: `macos.yml` builds the shell for Metal and runs `scripts/smoke.sh` against the job's instance where the SwiftUI app's build and smoke were, so the Mac app has a check on a Mac; `release.yml`'s macOS job builds a universal binary and runs `scripts/dist-macos.sh --release` (the same two commands made a universal `Lumia-0.3.0.dmg` here, signed ad hoc with no appcast, there being no credentials), with no delta from the releases up to 0.2.1, whose image is the SwiftUI app. Every job in every workflow has a timeout now, set from how long its recent runs took. **Neither has run**: both wait for a push, and the release job for a tag. Unknown until then: whether a runner's GPU draws the headless frames the smoke and the bundle check need.

Open, in the order to take it:
- [x] **Quote replies, reactions, mentions and read state** (architecture 6.6, decided with Ryan 2026-11-13; built 2026-11-13). In this order: the instance (the quote's reference and text with deletion's reach into replies, any-emoji reactions kept and counted, mentions of server members with notification only inside the channel, read state per account and conversation through the account log, mention counts and the Mentions query, badge counts and taking delivered notifications away), the protocol and the specs amended first; then the core; then the desktop shell and the web (Reply and the selection's Quote reply with the quote over the composer and in the timeline, jumping to the original; reaction chips, the quick row and the emoji picker; the composer's @ list with the add-them bar; bold, counts, the "New messages" line, the Mentions tiles); then iOS and Android by agents.
  - [x] The instance and the relay (2026-11-13). A quote resolves to its original before it is written (the author filled in, the text kept to 2,000 characters, `message_not_found` for one elsewhere, deleted or a notice), and deleting or purging the original drops the text from the replies' rows and stored events. A reaction is checked to be one emoji (`Lumia.Emoji`: one grapheme starting in the emoji blocks, or a keycap) on a message that is there, and kept as a row; snapshots and scrollback give each reader the counts, whether they are among them and the first fifty who reacted. A message keeps its mentions' places in its body and whom it notifies (those it names, the author it quotes, everyone); `@everyone` needs `MENTION_EVERYONE` on both write paths; push goes to those of them in the channel. `Lumia.Reads`: a position per account and conversation, started at the head on entering and backfilled to the head for everyone by the migration, moved forward only (`POST /channels/{id}/read`, `ReadPositionUpdated` on the account's log), counted per conversation for the snapshot's `read_states` (in a personal DM from its encrypted application events, whose event kind now tells them from commits and welcomes), the badge, and `GET /account/mentions`. Every notice carries the badge; a read wakes the account's other devices silently through the relay and UnifiedPush, and the relay passes a badge and the read kind and nothing else, sending a read to APNs as a background push. Checked by `mix precommit` (365 tests, 14 new) and the relay's tests (2 new). Not shown: a real APNs or FCM delivery of either kind.
  - [x] The core (2026-11-13). A message summary carries its quote, its mentions with their places, its reactions (count, whether this account is among them, who as far as known) and `mentions_me`; the store keeps all three (new columns, which an older store gains when it opens, and a reactions table in first-use order), materializes `ReactionAdded`/`ReactionRemoved`, and drops quote text when the original is deleted or purged. The personal DM projection keeps a `DmQuote` and drops its text the same way, and its summaries carry the quote and reactions. `App` has `send_message_with` (mentions and a quote), `react`, `dm_send_with`, `dm_react`, `mark_read`, `mentions`, `read_states` and `badge`: read state starts from the snapshot's `read_states`, counts what arrives after it (a message from someone else is unread, and notifies when it names this account or everyone, quotes it, or is in a DM; in a personal DM, every encrypted application event from someone else), and `ReadPositionUpdated` from another device clears a conversation. The wasm build has all of it for the web. Checked by the workspace's tests (a store test, two counter tests, a projection test) and a new live test against a scratch instance of this code (a quote of the member with a mention: unread and notifying, in Mentions; a reaction kept and a word refused; read, and the snapshot agrees). Not yet: a conversation gained after the snapshot has no read state until the next one; the FFI keeps the old surface until the phones' turn.
  - [x] The desktop shell (2026-11-13). The toolbar over the message under the pointer (the six quick reactions, Add reaction, Reply), "Quote reply" over a selection inside one message's body, the quote over the composer with Escape to cancel, quotes above messages (pressing one finds the original, reading back up to five pages, and flashes it; "Original message was deleted" when it was), reaction chips with who reacted, the emoji picker (search, "Frequently used" from this computer's reactions, Unicode's groups up to Unicode 16, since this Mac's emoji font has none of Unicode 18's and drew a box), mention pills, a tint on messages that mention this account, the composer's @ list (server members, "Not in #name", `@everyone` for those who may) and, after a send, the bar naming those mentioned who are not in the channel with "Add to #name" and who was invited instead. Unread rows in 600 and the strong ink with their counts, Mentions rows (a server's, and Home's across every server) with the Mentions pages, the rail's unread mark and counts, Home's count with the requests, the "New messages" line above the first unread message (scrolled to when above the fold), marking read while the newest message is on screen in front of the person, the Dock badge (`lumia-sparkle`'s `set_badge`) and a conversation's notice taken back once it is read here or elsewhere. `find_mentions` went into the core (and wasm) so every shell finds a body's mentions the same way. Checked by 8 new screen tests (198 in all), four new scenes drawn and looked at (`replies`, `mention-list`, `emoji-picker`, `mentions`, dark), and `scripts/smoke.sh` against a scratch instance of this code, whose first launch drew the line above the other account's message and whose second had nothing unread: the position moved end to end. The timeline bench, before and after: scrolling p50 0.145 → 0.167 ms (p95 0.33 → 0.58), a fling p50 0.53 → 0.74 ms, Cmd+A over 1,000 messages 0.6 → 12 ms, scrolling with that selection held p95 5.4 → 6.1 ms; the toolbar, the "Quote reply" button and the @ list are drawn at the root and not in the rows, and what remains is the one hover listener each row needs to say it is under the pointer. Not done: reaching a message's actions from the keyboard (the toolbar's buttons are out of the Tab order: eight stops a message would make the timeline impassable); the Dock badge and taking notices back have not run in a bundled app; nothing has been clicked in a real window; the picker builds every emoji when it opens (its first frame took 0.64 s headless, fonts loading in it), and should be a virtual list if that shows by hand.
  - [x] Found by Ryan in 0.6.0 (2026-11-13) and fixed: the desktop's message toolbar drew at the window's top left, not over the row under the pointer. It was anchored to the key path `rows/<id>`, and Rune finds an anchor by its exact key only, so it found none. A message's row now has a key of its own (`message-<id>`, apart from its slot's in the list, which holds a day's divider too) and the toolbar hangs from that; a test now says where the toolbar draws (the tests had pressed its buttons wherever they were), and the `message-toolbar` scene draws a hovered row.
  - [x] After the `v0.6.0` tag (2026-11-13): the release, the deploy and every check passed but the browser suite, where three steps failed. `history` never finished seeding (1,050 CLI processes on a runner took over four minutes); `send --count` now sends many over one connection and the step takes 16 s for both browsers here. `account` and `server settings` were refused their pictures ("That picture could not be read"), which was CI's and not this work's: in dev and test the instance's `priv` is a link to `apps/instance/priv`, so the compiled image library (`priv/native/liblumia_media.so`) is not under the cached `_build`, and a run that restores the cache finds the module compiled and no library to load, so every decode fails. The run before had missed the cache and built it. The e2e, macOS and instance workflows now cache `priv/native` with the build, keyed by the crate's sources.
  - [x] After the `v0.7.0` tag (2026-11-13): tagged once every workflow on master had passed (the iOS smoke's step limit raised from 10 minutes to 15 first: it took 6 to 9 before display settings and was cut off at 10 after). The deploy (the flagship reports 0.7.0), the web, Android, Linux and Windows passed; two jobs failed, so the release stayed a draft and was not published. The Mac app: Rune since `74a3c8d4` leaves `--snap` and its other headless runs out of a release build unless its `tooling` feature is on, and `bundle-macos.sh` checks the shipped binary by drawing a frame with it; the feature is on now (about 3.5 MB), the check passes here, and 0.7.1 carries it. The Linux job's frame check failed the same way unseen, being `continue-on-error`. The iOS archive, as at 0.6.1: the account's certificates are still at their maximum, which only Ryan can clear (the entry below). Done the same evening as `v0.7.1`: every job of its release passed, the iOS archive among them (Ryan revoked the stale certificates between the two tags; build 363 reached App Store Connect, and no TestFlight build reaches testers until the export-compliance question is answered, which is Ryan's), and it was published with the Mac disk image, its appcast offering 0.7.1 (build 363, with a delta from 322), the phones', Linux's and Windows'; the flagship reports 0.7.1. The 0.7.0 draft was deleted; its tag stays.
  - [ ] After the `v0.6.1` tag (2026-11-13): the Mac, Android, Linux and Windows builds, the web build and the deploy passed; three things failed. The browser suite's `history` step, as at 0.6.0 (its fix, `send --count`, was not yet pushed). The iOS archive, so 0.6.1 never reached TestFlight: "Your account has reached the maximum number of certificates". `release.sh` signs the archive automatically on a runner that starts with an empty keychain, so every release since 0.3.0 seems to have left one more Apple Development certificate in the account, whose key went with the runner. Ryan revoked the certificates the API key had made (2026-11-13); 0.7.0's iOS job still met the limit before that, and 0.7.1's uploaded build 363. To stop it recurring: one signing identity kept in the repository's secrets and imported into a keychain of the job's own, which needs Ryan. And the instance's load test missed a budget: send to delivered p99 141 ms against 100 (and 95 ms at 0.6.0, which passed), where fifteen runs before it read 8 to 21 ms. Not explained: here the commit before quote replies and 0.6.1 read the same (37.2 and 37.3 ms p99, p50 6.5 and 5.7 ms), the runner image and Postgres 18.7 were the same, a plain send's path gained nothing but cheap checks, and the two new indexes on `messages` are partial and skip it. Acks were as slow as deliveries, so the stall is in the write path. Next: the next push's run, and a Linux run of both commits in Docker if it is slow again.

  - [x] The instance (2026-11-13). Found on the way, and a hole in every release up to 0.6.1: an edit or a delete was checked only for the permission to write in the channel, so anyone who could post there could change or delete anyone's message. `Access.check_command` now checks the author for an edit, and the author or `MANAGE_MESSAGES` for a delete, which asks only `view_channel` at the gateway; 6 new tests. An edit's materialized row carries its mentions again (keeping the quoted author's, if the reply had notified them); a reply may notify nobody (`QuoteReply.silent`, the reply bar's "@ On" switch).

- [x] **Colours as the web draws them on a wide-gamut display** (2026-11-13, from Ryan: the desktop's colours "feel more intense" than the web's). The shell drew the tokens' exact values (a `--snap` of `channels` has `bg` and `rail` to the byte), but Rune's Metal surface set its `CAMetalLayer`'s colour space to `nil` on every configure, wiping the sRGB tag the window was made with, so macOS put the sRGB values on the panel unconverted. Every display here is wider than sRGB (the Studio Display is P3; the two BenQ RD280Us more so, sRGB red at 0.81 of their own), so every saturated colour drew too strong on all three. Fixed upstream the same day (Rune pull request 902, `53baebed`; its issue 540 is still open for the hover flicker it also names), and Rune moved from `1f1b6c22` to `74a3c8d4`, the head of `dev`, which repeats two more of its patched crates (`parley_engine`, `accesskit_windows`). Checked: format, clippy, every test, and `scripts/smoke.sh` against a scratch instance of this code (it fails against the dev instance on :4000 at either rev: that one has run since 2026-11-11 without reloading). Not checked: nobody has looked at a window since, so the colours matching the web is read from Rune's change and its measurements, not seen here. **The timeline bench found a regression in the new rev**: medians unchanged (scrolling p50 0.181 → 0.185 ms, a fling 0.81 → 0.79 ms) but the worst frames are not: scrolling's 0.77 → 59 ms, a fling's p99 1.6 → 58 ms, appending's 1.2 → 116 ms, and the first frame 7.8 ms → 3.46 s (fonts load in it). Committed as it is at Ryan's word, and the cause found the same evening: Rune's CoreText fallback (`text/system_fonts/core_text.rs`, from its pull requests 766 and 789, both before 902) asks, for each character the app's fonts lack, which family CoreText falls back to, and when that family is hidden reads every face's names in its file to find a public one. For a character the system font has (Cyrillic, say) the answer is `.AppleSystemUIFont`, hidden, so it read SFNS.ttf's 369 faces, about 55 ms, before seeing it was the system font and skipping it, once for every such character; a row bringing a new one paid it in its frame. Skipping the system font and families already met before that read brings the bench back to the old rev's (first frame 19.7 ms alone, scrolling's worst 0.82 ms, a fling's p99 1.5 ms, appending's worst 1.1 ms), with Rune's 111 text tests and Lumia's 205 passing and the `bodies` scene drawn pixel for pixel the same. Found by CI on the bump: the new rev claims a single instance on Linux too (a socket per process in the runtime directory), and `platform::instance::claim` asked it on every platform, so on Linux Rune's claim stood in front of the shell's own lock and socket and every claim after a process's first said another copy ran (4 of `tests/instance.rs` failed); the shell asks Rune on Windows alone now, as its module comment always said, and the five pass in an Ubuntu 24.04 container. And `who_is_typing_is_named_under_the_composer_until_their_time_runs_out` failed on the macOS runner, having allowed a second for the frames between a person's typing and the check (they took 1.85); it bounds the end by the moment before and the moment of the check now. Filed with the patch as Rune issue 916 (its pull requests are closed to outsiders): the shell runs the slow rev until Rune takes it. A launch has not been timed with `scripts/startup.py` since.
- [ ] TestFlight has had nothing new for testers since the first uploads (Ryan, 2026-11-13): every upload since 0.3.0 succeeded, and the builds most likely wait at "Missing Compliance", since the app's Info.plist does not answer the export question. Lumia implements standard algorithms (MLS through `openmls`, ChaCha20-Poly1305, X25519, Ed25519, rustls), so the answer is "Standard encryption algorithms", and Ryan is filing the French declaration to keep France. When Apple approves the documentation and gives an `ITSEncryptionExportComplianceCode`, it goes into `Lumia-iOS/project.yml`'s Info.plist with `ITSAppUsesNonExemptEncryption = YES`; until then each build is answered by hand in App Store Connect.

Now, on every client: the sections stand 12 apart; a server's private channels are a Private section of their own between Channels and Direct messages (no plus, since Create a channel makes one private), drawn only when there are any, as both Quiet Splits and the phone boards draw it; the phones' rows are 44, the headings only as tall as their words with the plus keeping its 44 target beyond them, and the iPhone's header has the board's 8 under it and its words 20 in (Home's too). Android's three links under the sections lost the extra padding around them. Checked: a desktop screen test (Private between the two, none without private channels), the `channels` scene in both themes, its 221 tests and `scripts/smoke.sh`; the web's gallery, rebuilt with the board's Private section; the iPhone 15 Pro simulator (iOS 17.4) by `scripts/smoke-membership.sh`, which passes and draws the sidebar with a private channel; Android on the emulator in both themes. (The lighter panel behind the sidebar's list in Ryan's Mac screenshot was his annotation.)

Two causes. (1) Neither phone had ever drawn a person's picture, only server icons; now both do wherever the desktop does (message rows and a reply's reference line, members, Add people, the server DM picker, the @ list, profile cards, server DM rows and headers, Home's rows and a personal conversation's header with one other person, requests, the rail's own avatar, and on iOS the Profile panel's header), by the desktop's rule: this account's from its snapshot, anyone else's from the servers' member lists, else from a profile the core's `account` call already looks up, and only pictures on the instance's own `/api/v1/avatars/` path (by agents, reviewed and cherry-picked; a unit test on each phone for the order; seen on the iPhone 15 Pro simulator through `scripts/smoke-ios.sh`, which now seeds two pictures, and on the emulator; `scripts/smoke-server-settings.sh` passes after `LumiaPictureOver` changed). (2) A fresh install drew replies, reactions and "New" (`scripts/smoke-ios.sh`), so Ryan's phone had messages kept by the builds before 0.6, which encoded each event again after decoding and so dropped what they did not know; nothing fetched them again. The core now marks what a store's rows keep (`MATERIALIZED`, 1) and a store marked lower or not at all fetches its server channels again at open, as a new device does; personal conversations are not touched (1ac2f89, a store test; the desktop's 221 tests). Every client refetches its server channels once on the first launch after this. Not done: setting one's own picture from either phone (the desktop has the Profile photo card); a personal conversation's pictures on a phone, which no smoke reaches (the phones sign in by password with no identity); dark theme and real devices for the pictures. Found and not a fault: Android lint flags `java.lang.ref.Cleaner` (API 33) in the generated bindings, which check for it at run time and fall back to JNA's cleaner.

- [x] **Show deleted messages, a server setting off by default** (GitHub #2; 2026-11-13, from Ryan: "show deleted messages should be a server setting, default to off. so when you delete a message, it doesn't show"). Architecture 6.7 amended first (251642d): off, a deleted message is gone from its conversation for everyone, its neighbours joining as if it had not been sent, and a reply to it still says "Original message was deleted"; on, it keeps its place as "(deleted)"; changed by whoever may change the server's settings, from any client, audited and told through the account log; the instance writes the same tombstone either way. A personal conversation, which no server governs, shows none. The instance keeps `show_deleted` on the server (a migration, `Server.show_deleted` and `UpdateServerRequest.show_deleted` in the protocol, a controller test). The core applies it so every client reads alike: `messages`, `messages_before`, `older_messages` and `dm_messages` leave deleted messages out where the channel's server does not show them, reading on through up to ten pages so a run of deletions never ends scrollback early, a channel read before joining following its server's say too (from what Browse and a server DM begun named of it; found by the Android agent); `ServerSummary.show_deleted` and `set_show_deleted` (wasm, FFI); unit tests and the live test, whose moderator's delete now takes the message away and, turned on, brings it back as deleted. Each client: "Show deleted messages" with "Off, a deleted message disappears for everyone. On, it stays in its place as “(deleted)”." at the end of the Message history card, saved as it is turned, and the open conversation read again when a snapshot changes a server's setting (desktop and web here; iOS and Android by an agent each, reviewed and cherry-picked). Checked: the instance's 372 tests; the core's tests and live suite; the desktop's 222 tests and a screen test of the switch; the browser suite's server settings step (the owner turns it on and the instance has it) and editing step (a deleted message leaves no "(deleted)") in both browsers; iOS `smoke-server-settings` and `smoke-replies` on iOS 17.4 (the delete leaves nothing; the seed's reply to a deleted draft still says "Original message was deleted"); Android on the emulator: a delete vanishing live with the setting off, "(deleted)" coming back live when it was turned on, a refusal putting the switch back. The iOS agent also found why `smoke-replies` failed at its edit step on iOS 17.4 (the editor that grows is a text view there; the display settings entry above records it) and fixed it; that half is done, and Return adding a line in the editor on iOS 27 was not looked at again. Not verified: the web drawing "(deleted)" with the setting on (the suite turns it on in another server than it deletes in); dark themes for the switch. Two commits on the way did not build alone and are left as they are, since another session works on this master: the instance's (14270c3) changed the protocol before the core's literal knew the field, and the core's (50977bb) before the desktop's. Also: the desktop's "(edited)" now follows a message's words on their last line, in the small muted type and left out of a copy, and stands on a line of its own only after code, a quote or a list (Ryan's screenshot with #2 showed it on a line of its own).
- [x] **The server rail's order and folders** (GitHub #6 and #7; 2026-11-14, decided with Ryan in a grill the night before, the boards approved with "make the server icons the same squared shape as the folders"). Architecture 6.9 written first (5d7e444), and amended once (62bfa2e): New folder from a menu opens Folder settings to name it, a drag does not. One arrangement per account at the instance, an ordered list of servers and folders (a client-minted id, an optional name of up to 50 characters, one of the nine avatar tones, its servers; no folder in a folder; at most 100), written whole, latest write wins, tidied by the instance (strangers and repeats dropped, empty folders gone, a server it leaves out last in the order joined); `PUT /account/rail`, the snapshot's `rail` and a `RailArranged` account event. Every client draws the same: server tiles, Home and + as rounded squares (radius 14), the account's own avatar round; a closed folder a tile in its tone with its first four servers small and their mentions added up (else the unread dot); an open one its glyph over its servers in a column of its tone, open or closed each device's own; a tile's top or bottom quarter between (a line in the accent), its middle half onto (a server makes a folder, a folder takes it in), a folder between only, Escape putting it back, on the phones a long press then a move; the menus (Move up, Move down, New folder, Move to folder, Remove from folder; a folder's Move up, Move down, Folder settings…, Delete folder); Folder settings with a name, nine swatches, Cancel and Save; a folder read as "Folder, <name or its servers>" with expanded or collapsed, and every move said aloud. The edits are the core's (`lumia_client::rail`, through FFI and wasm), so the four shells move tiles by one set of rules, and the core sorts a snapshot's servers by the rail, which every other list of servers follows. Built: the instance (baad913, 6b31591), the core (1a2deb4, 0630578, 93f29db), the desktop (1152450, b140970) and the web (83c6495, fe976c2, 0410b11) here; iOS (7065823) and Android (c541151) by an agent each, reviewed and cherry-picked; six icons and a right chevron in `assets/icons`.

Fixed (`f856622`, Ryan: "if not, please do"): `scripts/asc-certificates.rb --revoke` runs before the archive and revokes the development certificates named "Created via API" that earlier runs left, and nothing else. Shown by `ios-upload` on `2d7802e`: of 12 certificates it revoked those 10 and left Ryan's own development certificate and the Developer ID one; the archive passed and build 648 (0.13.3) went up to App Store Connect.

  - [x] `macos` had failed on each of its last eleven runs, at the WebKit browser suite or at the desktop's smoke (Ryan, 2026-11-16: "did you fix the issues that were causing CI to fail? if not, please do"). Both were real faults, not the runner's:
    - [x] **The web app's frame left the document and came back, twice, just after it was drawn, and took the keyboard from whatever had it** (`4134d0c`). A query made when its answer is already held says at once it has data, but Solid Query's resource takes that answer a moment later, and a read in between suspends the route: the same elements out and in again. On a fast machine that is over before anything is typed; on the runner it landed mid-step, so the words typed or a press went nowhere, at "sign in", "create", "replies", "rail" or "server settings" as the timing fell. Not reproduced here by loading every core, by a proxy answering the instance 1.5 s late, by a cold Vite cache or by background priority; found with a workflow that runs the suite in WebKit alone (`webkit.yml`, 3 of 3 runs failing) and the suite now saying where the keyboard is while a step waits ("the keyboard in body", no document loaded again), then here by logging every removal of the frame with its stack. `settled` in `client/state.ts` now keeps `data` undefined for two microtasks after a query is made, nothing drawn between; the frame is put in once, in Chromium and WebKit; the suite's sign-in step fails if the frame ever leaves ("left the document 2 times" without the fix). The whole suite passes in both engines against a scratch instance.
    - [x] **A device linked just before it connected never published its key packages**, so nobody could add it to a private conversation until its next connection (`e2f771b`, in the core, so every client). The DM engine is built on first use, and when an identity call held the identity's stores as the gateway started (a link just claimed), the gateway's welcome came with no engine to hear it. The welcome is kept now and the first engine built hears it. Against a scratch instance the desktop's live test waited for the key packages in vain in 2 runs of 3 before and passed 5 of 5 after. This is what `live_dm` ("the welcome into their group") and `live_notice` ("the notice") had been failing on.
    - [x] The desktop asked the engine for its conversations from a thread per ask, so an older answer could land over a newer one and a conversation just joined read as not joined; one ask at a time now, asked again when asked meanwhile (`639d542`). No test shows the race itself.
    - [x] The live tests wait for what they depend on instead of a fixed time or one read: this device's key packages (`Session::dm.published`, from the engine's note through `lumia_client::dm::says_published`), the other device's `dm open` in place of two seconds of `dm tail`, the welcome into the group, and the invite in the server's list (`live_servers.rs:150` had read it once). The fixed waits left in `live_notice` are for a notice that must not come.
    - [x] Seen on the runner at `4134d0c`: `macos` passed, for the first time in twelve runs, and `webkit` ran the whole suite three times with none failing, where three of three had failed before; core, web, e2e, shell and android passed there too.

  - [x] #15 Profiles: a changed name or picture reaches others only after a restart or reload (filed 2026-11-14, built the same day). **Instance**: a name, picture or signed profile change still puts `ProfileUpdated` on the account's own log in its transaction, and schedules `Lumia.Workers.ProfileUpdated`, which puts it (the account as others see it, no signed profile) on the log of every account sharing a server or a personal conversation with it, five hundred to a transaction, changes made while it waits being one job (as `ServerUpdated` does for a server); protocol 9's three rows say so. **Core**: writes the person into every kept member list (each server's nickname and switches kept) and emits `AppEvent::ProfileUpdated` after the `AccountApplied` it came in (UniFFI `ClientEvent.ProfileUpdated`, wasm `profileUpdated`). **CLI**: `account update --name … --picture …`. **Clients** (an agent each, brought together on one branch): each patches the person in place wherever it holds them (member lists, people, looked-up profiles, the snapshot when it is this account) without fetching a member list again, and everything that draws them reads from there; a new picture is a new address, fetched as any is, a cleared one is the initials. Checked: the instance's worker tests and suite (383); the core's (133); the desktop's windowless `tests/profiles.rs` (four, failing without the handler) and its smoke with the new `tests/live_profiles.rs` against a scratch instance; the web's unit tests and the browser suite's new profile step in Chromium and WebKit against a scratch instance running this branch (a member renamed themself and set a picture, then another, and their message and the member list drew each at once with no reload); iOS's kit tests (a session-level one among them) and a dev scene's before and after; Android's JVM tests. Live through the real job queue: a CLI rename reached the account sharing a server and not a stranger. Not checked: the phones against an instance; a picture's bytes drawn on a phone. **Found on the way**: every client draws the display name before a server nickname (the nickname only where there is no display name), which may not be what a nickname is for, a decision for Ryan; three more, **fixed the same day**: Android and iOS fetched every server's member list again on each `AccountApplied` (a read on another device was enough), and now read lists only for servers newly in the snapshot, or for all of them on a welcome, when a reconnect may have missed something; the web's patch could be overwritten by a member-list fetch already in flight, and now each fetch notes where the updates stood as it began and brings its answer up to date with those after (`profileLog`, with unit tests); the core's `identity_profile` preferred this device's own signed profile, so a Profile page on a device that ever signed one kept that name after a change elsewhere, and now shows the newer of this device's and the instance's, and when the instance's is as new, the account's name and picture as they are (`shown_profile`, with unit tests). Not checked: the phones' reads and the Profile page against an instance.
  - [x] #16 Desktop (Linux): the emoji picker drew empty boxes instead of emoji (filed 2026-11-14, fixed the same day). Cause, in Rune at the pinned rev: on Linux it takes its emoji from one family, "Noto Color Emoji", found by name; its fontconfig fallback asks per script and skips the Common script every emoji is in; and it tells the emoji family by "Emoji" in its name, which Twemoji fails. Ryan's machine has Twemoji only, so no emoji font loaded. Fixed in Lumia (Ryan's choice, 2026-11-14): Linux builds carry Google's COLRv1 Noto Color Emoji (`apps/desktop/fonts/`, OFL, 2 MB as WOFF2 and 5 MB in the binary), which `build.rs` compiles into the app's fonts off Apple and Windows, and Rune finds it among them by the name it looks for, whatever the system has; the .deb no longer recommends `fonts-noto-color-emoji`. Checked on this Mac with a copy of Rune whose Mac emoji family was pointed at that name: the picker drew Noto's smileys, the gendered runners whole, the facing-right runners and the flags, its first frame 584 ms against Apple's 644. `linux_builds_carry_the_emoji_face_rune_looks_for` (tests/replies.rs) runs on Linux only, so CI is its first run. Not checked: the app on a Linux desktop (Ryan's, after the next release). Reported upstream as anomalyco/rune#973 (2026-11-14: ask fontconfig for its generic `emoji` family, as Rune asks for `monospace`, and tell the emoji family by its colour tables); when Rune fixes it, Lumia can stop carrying the font so a person's own emoji font is used. Rune's #103 (COLRv1 draws nothing) is still open but fixed in practice since its #764: the bundled COLRv1 font draws through it.
  - [ ] Found on the way (2026-11-14), macOS: the 18 "facing right" people of Unicode 15.1 (🏃‍➡️ and the walking, kneeling, cane and wheelchair ones) draw as nothing in the picker; the older 🤜 beside them draws. Apple Color Emoji has them since macOS 14.4, so this is Rune shaping or drawing a ZWJ sequence that ends in ➡️ with Apple's font; Noto's draw through the same Rune. Not looked into further. Until it is, the picker could leave them out on the Mac.
  - #17 Servers: custom emoji, with a permission for who can upload them (filed 2026-11-14). Settled 2026-11-20 and carried by step 12.
  - [~] #20 Private messages: no way to start, since linking needs a device that already has keys (filed 2026-11-14; decided with Ryan the same day, architecture 5.3, 5.4, 6.3 and 11.4 and identity 4.1, 4.7, 7 and 10 amended). Every client sent a device without an identity to "Link this computer"; only `lumia-cli identity create` or `identity recover --phrase` could make or restore one. Decided: an account has an identity from sign-up (native apps create it silently, the phrase follows with reminders until three words are confirmed; the web confirms in the flow), "Already use Lumia?" leads to link or restore, an account met without one is set up at its next sign-in, the phrase is the only recovery secret (no PIN), the web restores only on the flagship's origin. Telling no identity from one elsewhere is `Account.identity_id`.
    - [x] Docs: architecture, identity, e2ee and protocol amended (2026-11-14).
    - [x] Instance: reauth by passkey assertion, mailed code (`Lumia.Auth.ReauthCodes`) and OIDC login (`Oidc.start_reauth/2`, the callback checking the subject and `auth_time`), beside password and TOTP; a reauth token in the `TokenPair` of a sign-in that proved a credential, and a handoff passing on its session's for five minutes without renewing it (2026-11-14; identity §12 cases 14 and 15 tagged, `reauth_api_test.exs`, `oidc_test.exs`). Not run against a real OIDC provider, only the test one; whether real providers send `auth_time` for `max_age=0` is the open part.
    - [x] Instance: `POST /identity/rebind` (`Identity.rebind_account/2`): the old roster's devices lose their sessions and key packages on the account, its signed profile goes, `IdentityRebound` on its log and `ContactIdentityChanged` on its contacts'; an identity bound to another account is `ALREADY_EXISTS`, an unbound account `FAILED_PRECONDITION` `unbound` (2026-11-14; identity §12 case 16 tagged).
    - [x] Found on the way and fixed (2026-11-14): `POST /identity/link` took any session's reauth token, another account's included, so someone holding a stolen access token and a reauth token of their own could bind the victim's account to their identity. Link, rebind and revoke now take only the calling session's token (revoke checked the account, not the session). Test: `identity_api_test.exs`, "linking takes only this session's reauth".
    - [x] Core (2026-11-14): `App::identity_setup` (an identity made here unless held, the account bound under the reauth the sign-up or sign-in carried, the backup written; `AccountHasIdentity` when the account has one elsewhere, `ReauthNeeded` without a token), `identity_phrase`, `identity_phrase_unconfirmed`, `identity_phrase_quiz` and `identity_phrase_confirm` (a native device keeps the entropy until three words are right; `Phrase::quiz` and `Phrase::answers` in `lumia-core`), `identity_new_phrase` (the web's, held in memory and confirmed before setup), `identity_recover` and `identity_start_over`, created and recovered as WEB devices on the web with the backup key forgotten after; reauth by password, TOTP, mailed code or an OIDC token (`reauth_*`); all on the UniFFI and wasm surfaces. Sign-up is the instance's own creation then setup, which binds by link: one path on every instance, whatever its identity-proof policy. Tested live against a scratch instance (`live_app.rs`, three tests; identity §12 case 17 is the first of them).
    - [x] Core: `ContactIdentityChanged` is a roster change to the DM engine, so the old identity's leaves go at the next commit, with a note on the sink (`DmNote`, "started over with a new identity"); the new device rejoins by external commit (2026-11-14; `live_app.rs`, `a_contact_who_starts_over_is_told_and_the_conversation_goes_on`, three runs green). A conversation shows no lasting key-change marker yet: that is the boards' and the shells' part.

Architecture 5.2 says clients pin the key on first contact and warn on any change that is not a rotation; the DM engine judges leaves against whatever roster the instance serves for an account, so an instance that served a different identity's roster for a contact would be believed. Starting over makes the gap visible but did not open it. Needs its own piece of work: pins in the identity backup (identity 8 already lists them), the warning, safety numbers on screen.

    - [ ] A browser, and maybe every client, keeps its identity across a sign-out, so the next account signed in there meets an identity it is not bound to (found in the same review). The instance now refuses binding it, but the web still reads the case as its own: `unbound` sends it straight to binding, `mismatched` says "This browser holds the new identity from starting over", and the reauth before either says "this browser keeps the phrase you checked". Needs a decision: the core telling the shell the identity held is another account's (whose, without a lookup, is the question), or sign-out on a browser forgetting the identity (it is then gone unless the phrase was kept). To check on iOS, Android and the desktop: whether sign-out keeps the identity, and that each handles `ALREADY_EXISTS` from setting up. A unique partial index on `accounts.identity_id` would make one account per identity on an instance a guarantee rather than three checks; not added, since a database that already holds two rows for one identity would fail the migration on deploy.

The issue is the checklist of what "Linux updates, not shown" above lists: the `packages` job's first run and the site serving, an AppImage updating itself in a real window, under FUSE, on x86-64, the feed through GitHub's `latest/download`, apt and dnf against the real site; and whether a release with a failed Linux job should leave AppImages checking a missing feed.
  - [x] #18 Messages: long messages, code blocks and long pastes on every client (decided with Ryan 2026-11-14, built the same day, released in `v0.9.0` and closed; the issue has the checklist). Found first: no size limit was enforced anywhere (the instance advertised `max_message_bytes` 16,384 and nothing checked or read it; the gateway's frame is 1 MiB); only the desktop drew the Markdown subset; the web dropped line breaks and its composer was a single-line input, so a pasted block lost them before sending. Built:
    - The instance refuses a body over 16,384 bytes on send and edit with `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE` and `details.max`, over the gateway and HTTP (a link bundle's `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE`, which answered 500, is 413 now). The core holds the `Welcome`'s limit (`App::max_message_bytes`), refuses a longer body before sending (`ClientError::TooLong`), personal DMs included, and counts a draft (`body::room`: characters as grapheme clusters, from 90%, below it only the bytes left).
    - The grammar moved from the desktop into the core (`lumia_core::body`), the one implementation protocol 16 asked for: `parse_with` reads a body with its mention ranges (a mention is one unit, so a name's underscores are not Markdown; none inside code or a link), and `display` gives the web, iOS and Android its blocks in UTF-16 (UniFFI `parseBody`, wasm `parseBody`, attached by the web's worker to every message it hands the page). The desktop draws its pills from those ranges now rather than finding a name's words again.
    - On all four: the subset drawn (paragraphs keep their lines and break a long word; emphasis, strong, strike, inline code, links, quotes, lists); a code block never wraps and scrolls sideways inside itself, never up and down, with Copy (on the desktop and the web in its corner under the pointer or with focus; on a phone always, in a strip of its own over the code with the fence's language, as the canvas's long-message boards draw it), "Copied" for 1.5 s and said to a screen reader; a body over 14 drawn lines shows its first 10 fading into the row's own ground, with "Show more" / "Show less", opened per conversation visit; every composer grows to 8 lines and then scrolls (the web's is a textarea now; the desktop's `max_h` had never held, and a long paste covered the conversation); a counter from 90% ("1,204 characters left", "N characters too long" in the danger ink, said once on going over), and over the limit Send dimmed and refused and the draft kept; the edit field counts and refuses the same, at its own cap (8 on the desktop, 12 on the phones, half the window on the web). Syntax colouring stays the desktop's.
    - The canvas has the boards (2026-11-14): "Long messages and code", "The message limit", "iOS · Long messages", light and dark.
    - Checked: the instance's tests (send, edit, gateway, HTTP); the core's; the desktop's (`tests/body.rs`, `shell.rs`, `replies.rs`), its smoke and its scene `long-messages` against a scratch instance; the web's browser suite in Chromium and WebKit against a scratch instance, with a long step (thirty lines folded to ten and opened, a code block's long line kept and copied, a paste's lines kept, a draft a character over refused and one at 16,384 sent); `swift test` and the Android unit tests; each client's screens looked at in light and dark (the phones through debug sample screens, `LUMIA_DEV_SCREEN=bodies`). The CLI against a scratch instance: 16,384 bytes acked, 16,385 refused with code 7.
    - Not checked: a phone against an instance (a real long message, a send or edit refused over the limit); VoiceOver and TalkBack (the announcements and the expand actions are read from the code); a screen reader on the desktop or the web beyond the live region in the tree; the desktop's sideways scroll with a real trackpad, and on Windows and Linux. Rune gives a sideways viewport no scroll bar and no arrow keys at the pinned rev, so on the desktop a keyboard reads the rest of a long line only by selecting it.
    - Found in review and left: the desktop predicts a body's height from its blocks (`Body::height`, `timeline::fold_of`) because a virtual row is sized before it is laid out, so a change to its spacing must be made there too; the counter's words and the fold's 14 and 10 are written in each client (the core has the room, not the words); Copy and Show more rebuild the conversation's mounted rows through `session.revision`, as every row action does.
  - [x] #25 Messages and people: a menu on a right-click (filed 2026-11-18 from a tester's "side note id love to be able to reply etc via context menu instead of aiming carefully to get the corner hover over the right message.. same for the actions currently hidden in the user card too"; Ryan, the same day: "work on issue #25"; built on the desktop and the web that day; released in `v0.14.0` and closed 2026-11-20, Ryan: "Close the issue"). A message's actions were its toolbar's alone, at the corner of the row under the pointer, and a person's were inside their profile card; a right-click did nothing on either, and the only context menus were the rail's.
    - Decided. The issue asked for the menus and left three things open, answered here; say if one should go the other way. **Web: the browser's own menu stays** on a link, a picture, a video or audio element, a field being typed in, and text that was selected before the press, so "Open in new tab", "Copy link", "Save image" and Copy are still there; everywhere else on a message the app's menu comes up. **Desktop: a right-click on selected text offers it**, since a row with a menu takes the place of Rune's own Copy for the text under it: the menu leads with "Copy selection" when the message is in the timeline's selection (whatever the selection spans), with "Quote reply" when the selection is inside that message alone; and, there being no browser's menu behind it, a right-click on a link leads with "Open link" and "Copy link". **Phones: no long press on a person.** A tap already opens their sheet with its actions one tap away, and nothing on a phone hides behind a hover; it can follow if testers ask. Also decided: a message's menu is Reply, Add reaction, a rule, Edit message, Copy text, Delete message, offered by the toolbar's rules on each client (none on a notice or a deleted message; on the desktop none while a message is being edited, as it has no toolbar then; on the web none on the one being edited); a person's is "View profile" and then what their card offers where they are met, and this account's own is "View profile" alone; on a message's author the person's menu comes up, not the message's; a conversation's row has the person's menu only when it is with one other person; no quick reactions in the menu and no action the toolbar or the card does not have.
    - Built: **desktop** (`app/context.rs`, `ui/replies.rs`, `ui/menus.rs`): rows, avatars, names, member rows and conversation rows take Rune's `on_secondary_press`, which the Menu key and Shift+F10 reach too from the control the keyboard is on; the menu hangs from a place with no size drawn at the root where it was asked for (`context::AT`), and the emoji picker after it for Add reaction; the items call what the toolbar and More actions call (`own_actions` is drawn by both menus), and the card and the menu ask one `State::ways_to`; a server DM asked for from the menu and refused opens the card, which says why; the timeline tracks its whole selection (`replies.selected`) in the one pass that finds a quotable one. Scenes `message-context`, `message-context-selection`, `person-menu`. **Web** (by an agent in a worktree from the same decisions, reviewed and cherry-picked): `ContextMenu`, `MessageContextMenu` and `PersonMenu` in the design system and the gallery, a popover placed at the pointer and kept in the window (`client/menus.ts`: `placedAt`, `browsersMenu`, `personOffers`, which the card draws from too; `menus.test.ts`); the message's menu is `Timeline`'s own state like More actions, the person's is `Over` kind `personMenu`; mention pills have the person's menu too (the desktop's are not pressable); Safari selects the word under a right-click before it sends the event, so what was selected and what had the keyboard are read at the press and the word is let go when the menu is the app's. The web's menus have no glyphs and the person's has a line with their name at its head, each as that client's other menus are.
    - Found on the way, fixed: on the web **a popover never took the keyboard as it opened** (`Popover` asked for focus while its box was still hidden), so a menu's arrow keys did nothing until Tab; every popover takes it now (menus, the profile card, the picker, Folder settings, "Delete this message?", whose Cancel has it).
    - Checked: the desktop's format, clippy and 361 tests, `tests/context.rs` among them (twelve: the items by the rules and in order, the menu at the pointer, each item's effect with the clipboard read, a selection in one message and across three, a link right-clicked by the gesture, no menu on a notice, a deleted message or during an edit, a member's row, one's own, the card's ways and the refusal that opens it, an author's avatar and name, a server DM's row, the Menu key and Shift+F10 on a row, a reaction and an avatar) and Home's row and a group's in `tests/home.rs`; its smoke and every live test against a scratch instance from the tree; the three scenes looked at; the timeline bench before and after in both layouts (scroll, fling, append and tick within run-to-run noise; with a 1,000-message selection held a frame costs what reading the selection costs, as before). The web's check, 164 unit tests and build; its whole browser suite in Chromium and WebKit against the same instance, with a `menus` step (the items on another's message and on one's own, Reply, Copy text, Add reaction, Edit and Delete asked and at once with Shift, a link keeping the browser's menu, a member's row and an author's name, View profile, Shift+F10 for both, a right-click outside moving from one menu to the other); the agent also drove both menus in both browsers on a throwaway page before that.
    - Not checked: either menu by a person's hand in a real window or browser, a trackpad's two-finger click and Control-click among them; the desktop on Windows and Linux; a screen reader on either; Firefox, real Chrome on a Mac and a touch long-press on the web; the web's conversation rows (a server DM's and a personal one's) by anything but the typechecker; the desktop's "Open link" reaching a browser (the call is the one a press on a link makes). The suite's storage-limit step checked nothing on this instance, as it says without `LUMIA_SERVER_STORAGE_MAX_BYTES`.
    - Open: **a message with no control inside it is still not reached from the keyboard on the desktop.** The keys open a message's menu from its author, a reaction, a link or a quote's line; a plain message in a block has none of those, and a row is not a Tab stop (one a message would make the timeline impassable). Moving through messages with the arrow keys is the piece that would close it, here and for the toolbar. The design canvas has no board for either menu; the web's gallery shows both.
  - [x] #26 Messages appear at once, with no slide-up (filed 2026-11-18 by bkilshaw, built the same day). On the desktop a press or a key that brought rows (opening a channel, a send) grew each open on Rune's growth-stack spring, the pinned list sliding up under them for half a second, and faded the list in where there had been none; a send from further up glided to the bottom. Android followed a new message with `animateScrollToItem`. The web and iOS had no such motion and are unchanged.

The desktop's switch is wider than new rows: any change a commit makes inside a row ("Show more" unfolding, the edit field opening, reaction chips) lands at once too, since a new row and a row changing height ride the same growth track; Ryan was told and took it. Left as they were: the glide of a jump to a message (both clients), hover and the jump's flash, everything outside the list.
