  - [x] #24 Zoom in the picture viewer (filed 2026-11-18 from a tester's "and zoom on the inline image viewer (for images bigger than viewport)"; Ryan, the same day: "take on #24 now"; built on the core and all four clients that day; pushed and closed the same day, Ryan: "commit and push and close the issue"). The viewer zoomed on every client already, each by its own rules: the web to 4 times fitted, the desktop to 8 by three word buttons and the keys alone, iOS to 4 and Android to 6, every limit counted from the fitted size, so the largest display picture on a phone stopped short of its own size.
    - Decided (specs/media.md 5, "The viewer's zoom"): one set of rules, in `lumia_core::viewer`. A picture opens fitted, shrunk into the viewer and never grown; it zooms to four times its own size; a zoom is about the pointer or the fingers; a press on it (two taps on a phone) goes to its own size about that point, or to twice fitted when its own is under one and a half times that, and back; the buttons and keys step by one and a half; a drag stops at the picture's edges and a side that fits stays centred. **Own size is a picture pixel to a point** (a CSS pixel, a dp), not to a device pixel as the issue's words have it: on a dense display a fitted display picture (2,560 px at most) is usually past a device pixel already, so that reading would leave a large picture nothing to zoom to. **The wheel zooms** on the web and the desktop, a trackpad's two fingers included, as the issue asks and the web did; a pinch zooms as well, and what a trackpad sends after the fingers lift is left out on the desktop.
    - Built: **core** `viewer.rs` (a frame from a picture's size and its room, `zoom_at`, `pan_by`, `held`, `step`, `pressed`, `own_size`, `wheel_factor`), `proto/vectors/viewer/cases.json` (5 frames, 24 moves, 7 wheel events) with `tests/viewer_vectors.rs`, `App::viewer_*` and the FFI's `viewer_frame`, `viewer_zoom_at`, `viewer_pan_by`, `viewer_pressed`, `viewer_held`. **Desktop** (`app/media.rs`, `ui/media.rs`): the wheel and a pinch about the pointer (Rune has `on_wheel` and `on_magnify`; off macOS a pinch is a wheel with Control held), a press on the picture, a drag held at its edges where it had no limit, the bar's −, Fit, 1:1 and + (the button drawn "1:1" used to fit the window), the keys + − 0 1; the scene `media-viewer-zoomed`. **Web**: `client/viewer.ts`, the rules written again since a wheel event cannot wait for the worker, held to the vectors by `viewer.test.ts`; Fit and 1:1 in the bar, the 1 key, a pinch (Control with the wheel) counted as one, and on a touch screen two fingers followed, which a phone's or a tablet's browser had no zoom by but a tap and the buttons. **iOS** (by an agent in a worktree): `ViewerZoom` in LumiaKit over the FFI and a `UIScrollView` subclass that draws the picture at its fitted size, takes its limit and its two-tap view from the core, keeps a side that fits centred and refits when the screen turns; debug scenes `LUMIA_DEV_SCREEN=viewer` and `viewer-small` and `scripts/smoke-viewer.sh`, which measures them. **Android** (by an agent in a worktree): `Zoom` in the kit (`Viewer.kt`) over the FFI, a pinch about the fingers' centroid where it was about the middle, two taps in as well as back, a drag with limits, the picture at its fitted size; `scripts/core.sh` now also builds `lumia-ffi` for the machine it runs on in a debug build (`.build/host`), so the kit's unit tests ask the core itself through JNA (`ViewerTest`).
    - Found on the way and fixed: **the web** took the click that ends a drag as a press, so moving a zoomed picture fitted it again (the e2e's media step now drags a zoomed picture and checks it stays zoomed); **Android's viewer dialog** was laid out as tall as the screen and placed under the status bar, its last 136 px cut off on the emulator, which a drag with limits could never have reached: the dialog is the whole screen now, black to its edges, and pads itself clear of the bars; **iOS and Android** grew a picture smaller than the screen to its width.
    - Checked: the core's format, clippy and 261 tests; the desktop's format, clippy, 345 tests (three new in `tests/media.rs`: the wheel, a pinch and momentum through Rune's own events, a press and a drag, the buttons and keys to the limit) and `scripts/smoke.sh` against a scratch instance from the tree, with `media-viewer` and `media-viewer-zoomed` drawn headlessly; the web's check, 136 tests, build, the gallery's viewer driven in Chromium (fitted 723 wide of 1,600, 1,600 at its own size, 6,400 at the limit, a drag stopped at the picture's edge; two fingers sent through Chromium's touch input spread a picture to four times and moved it with them) and the e2e's media step in Chromium and WebKit against that instance; iOS's LumiaKit tests (210), the test build and `smoke-viewer.sh` on an iPhone 15, an iPhone 18 Pro and an iPad with the screen turned, and the real viewer in `smoke-media.sh`'s picture test; Android's unit tests (175, `ViewerTest` 9) and debug build, and by hand on an API 35 emulator against a scratch instance: three pictures fitted, two taps, a drag to the corner, pinches sent as two fingers through `sendevent`, a GIF.
    - Not checked: any of it by a hand on a real trackpad, mouse or phone (every pinch and wheel was sent by a test); the web's two fingers in WebKit or on a real touch screen, and one finger lifting while the other drags on, which Chromium's touch input would not send; the desktop on Windows and Linux, where a pinch is a wheel with Control held and Rune's line step differs; the Android workflow's new host build of the core on Linux, neither reproduced in a container nor timed (the job has its 45 minute limit); Android before 11 and a side cutout with the whole-screen dialog; a picture with no stated size on the phones; a screen reader on any client; Android's `scripts/smoke.sh`.
    - Found and left, not from this work (the same on the sources before it): **iOS's `smoke-media.sh` fails in `testVideoPlaysInPlaceAndEmbedsLoadOnAPress`**, at two lines for two reasons. `SmokeUITests.swift:1633` ("a drag over the Bluesky post did not scroll the conversation") is the test's geometry: the live Bluesky post it shows is now 527 pt tall, its top 35.7 pt above the screen, so a drag begun at 30% of its height lands a point above the list, on the header; a drag begun on the post where it is on screen scrolls the conversation 386 pt, so the rule holds and the test wants its start point moved. `:1634` looks like a fault in the app: **while a Bluesky post is shown, a drag begun on that message's own words, its gutter or the embed's bar does not scroll the list** (five starts on that row moved nothing in the iOS 17.4 simulator, while a drag begun on the next message's card scrolled from the same place). The mechanism is not found; the row is the one with a `WKWebView` in it (`EmbedHost`, `Playback.swift`) under the row's long press (`Components.swift`). Not checked on a device or for X's and YouTube's embeds. The third test of that script (Images as links) does not run past the failure.
    - Open: the players' two full-screen dialogs in Android's `Playback.kt` are set up as the viewer's was and may be cut off at the foot the same way (not looked at); a wheel zooms a picture one of whose sides still fits by a little off the pointer until that side fills the room, since a fitting side stays centred; the issue's first question, which client the tester was on, is still Ryan's to ask.
  - [x] #23 Each conversation keeps what is typed in it (filed 2026-11-16 from a tester's "typing but not sending still clears the input if you switch to another channel mid-composition - if it had a memory as you type, it could completely quit (or die in a power cut) and it still be there waiting to send on relaunch"; Ryan, 2026-11-18: "next work on #23"; built on the core and all four clients that day; pushed and closed the same day, Ryan: "commit and push and close the issue when done"). Every client held the composer's words in the screen's own state and wrote them nowhere: the desktop emptied its one draft at every change of conversation, the web kept one draft for whatever channel was open, and the phones lost theirs with the screen.
    - Decided (architecture 13, "Local-first"): a draft is the conversation's, kept in the core's instance store on that device, never sent to the instance. The issue left two things to decide. **The reply being written is kept with the words** (each client holds it as the core's `QuoteSummary`, so it costs a column; the reply bar's "@ On" switch travels with it as the quote's `silent`); **the files waiting in the composer's strip are not** (they are uploads in flight, held by the session, and have no kept form). **No mark in the sidebar** for a conversation with a draft: no board draws one, and it can follow. Words that are empty or all blank keep nothing, the reply neither, which is how a send and an emptied field clear a draft. Signing out, a sign-in in a held session's place and a removed device's erasure forget every draft, and from a sign-out or an erasure until the next sign-in the core keeps none.
    - Built: **core**: a `drafts` table by channel id, `App::draft` and `App::set_draft` (the first 64 KiB of the words, cut between characters), forgotten by `forget_account`, by `take_session` over a held session and by `erase`, with both bindings. **Every client** reads a conversation's draft when it comes under the composer, at a launch too, keeps what is typed once it has rested 400 ms, at once when the conversation leaves and when the app or page goes behind or away, and keeps nothing at a send; words typed before the kept draft has been read stay and take its place. **Desktop**: `app/drafts.rs` (`State::tick` looks, since the composer has no hook for an edit; `draft_wake` wakes the window to keep rested words; `leave_draft` at every change of conversation; `on_close` and `on_quit`, which Rune has for this, and the window losing focus). **Web**: `client/drafts.ts` (`DraftKeeper`, and `keepDrafts` for the channel's composer and the personal conversation's; kept too when the page is hidden or goes). **iOS** (by an agent in a worktree): `DraftKeeper` in LumiaKit and a `Drafting` modifier on both conversation screens; a send that fails now puts the words and the reply back, as the web does, where it dropped them. **Android** (by an agent in a worktree): `DraftKeeper` in the kit, `rememberDraft` on both screens, reads and writes on one serial dispatcher so a save handed over as a screen leaves is in the store before the next screen reads; the draft is no longer `rememberSaveable`, the store being its one source.
    - Found on the way: **a save made on the way out of an account could outlive the sign-out** (the web's composer keeps its words as it is torn down, after the sign-out that forgot the drafts; Android's and iOS's agents each saw the same window at a sign-out or an erasure), closed in the core, which keeps nothing between a sign-out or an erasure and the next sign-in. **Left**: a sign-in over a held session forgets the drafts but, read from `take_session`, not the outbox, so commands the first account had not sent may go out at the next welcome as whoever signed in (not tried; `forget_account` clears it only at a sign-out); Android still drops the words of a send that fails, where the web and now iOS put them back; `SmokeUITests`' `composer(in:)` asks every element for its identifiers and crashed the app under test on iOS 17.4 when polled often (the new tests use a typed lookup, the old ones still use it); a fresh Android emulator's first tap into a field opens Gboard's stylus sheet, which swallows what `smoke.sh` types next.
    - Checked: the core's format, clippy and 265 tests (a draft read by a second launch on the same files, emptied, cut at the limit, forgotten by a sign-out and not kept after it); the desktop's format, clippy, 348 tests (`tests/drafts.rs`: two channels' words and replies, a rested save and a second launch on the same directory, an emptied field, a sign-out) and `scripts/smoke.sh` twice against a scratch instance, whose live test now keeps a draft once rested, sends from the composer and finds nothing kept, and finds the next one at a second launch; the web's check, 141 tests, build and the whole e2e in Chromium and WebKit against that instance, with a new drafts step (a reply half written in one channel, another channel's draft, both back after a reload, a send and an emptied field keeping nothing); iOS's LumiaKit tests (222) and `smoke-drafts.sh` on iOS 27 and 17.4 simulators (a switch, an edit beside a draft, `kill -9` and a launch, a send, a personal conversation); Android's unit tests (185, a real client on a temporary directory among them), debug build and the whole `smoke.sh` on an API 35 emulator, with a force-stop after a second, a rotation and the "@ On" switch by hand.
    - Not checked: a real quit of the desktop app's window or a power cut (the close, quit and focus hooks are wired and not driven; the rested save is what a test shows); the desktop on Windows and Linux; the web's personal conversation composer and its sign-out in a browser (the keeper and the core's refusal are tested, the screen is not); iOS's flush on leaving the foreground and its failed-send put-back on screen; Android's personal conversation and server DM (the same wiring as the channel that was seen); a draft waiting when a session is ended from elsewhere; a screen reader on any client; a real phone. The phones were driven on the core before its last commit (the refusal after a sign-out); their unit tests were run again on it.
    - Open: a mark for a conversation with a draft, once a board draws one; the files waiting in the strip; drafts of a channel left or deleted stay in the store until the account signs out (nothing reads them).
  - [ ] #22 Get rid of image thumbnails (filed 2026-11-16 by Ryan: "Let's just use the optimized images."; built 2026-11-18, then taken back the same day on Ryan's word, "okay revert and fix the decode sizes"; the issue is still open, his to close or reword). **Thumbnails stay.** What made them look bad on a phone was not the thumbnail: iOS decoded a message's picture at 800 px and Android at 540 (its decoder samples by halves to under an edge, which was 1,024), both sizes left from when thumbnails were 400 px, so the raise to 1,080 on 2026-11-15 never reached a phone's screen.
    - Fixed: iOS decodes a message's picture at `MediaInput.thumbnailEdge` and Android at `THUMBNAIL_EDGE`, both 1,080, a thumbnail's own size, so one is drawn with every pixel it was sent with; Android's rule is a plain value now (`sampleFor`), a personal card's picture is decoded at the same edge (the core makes it up to 1,080 and it was halved at 800), and its decoded-picture cache is 64 MB, from 32, a whole thumbnail being four times a halved one.
    - What removing them looked like, for whoever asks again (`ea9bc42`, reverted by `4a3d7c2`): one blob a picture and every client drawing the display picture, checked on all four against a scratch instance. It was taken back for what it costs: a picture in a conversation becomes its whole display blob to fetch, up to 2 MiB where a thumbnail is at most 512 KiB; a desktop or an Android build from before it draws a new picture as its placeholder or a broken-picture glyph until it updates; and Rune keeps what it is handed decoded whole, some 26 MB for a display picture by arithmetic, so the desktop had to hold each made small by the core. The decode sizes gave most of the sharpness for none of that.
    - Checked: `swift test` (206; `testAThumbnailIsDecodedWholeForAMessage` is new) and the iOS test build; Android's unit tests (`aThumbnailIsDecodedWholeAndALargerPictureByHalves` is new) and debug build, on a core rebuilt from the reverted tree; the desktop's format and clippy and its `media-viewer` scene drawn. Not checked when this was committed: either phone's media smoke on the decode change (iOS's was running, Android's not yet started; both had passed earlier the same day on the build without thumbnails), and nobody has compared a camera's picture before and after by eye on a phone.
    - Left: on the desktop a 1,080 px thumbnail is drawn up to 1,200 px wide at two pixels a point (600 by 400 points), a little enlarged and compressed twice, so a wide picture is slightly soft there; a larger thumbnail (1,600, with a larger cap) would end it and has not been decided. iOS plays a GIF in place from frames of at most 400 px (`AnimatedImage.frames`, at most 200 of them, which is what bounds their memory), so a GIF is soft on a phone while its still first frame is sharp. The core makes a personal card's picture at a thumbnail's 1,080 where spec 6.3 says 800 (`card_thumbnail` goes through `process`); nothing draws it wrong now, and the spec or the core should be made to agree.
    - Found beside it and fixed: the desktop's `media-viewer` scene opened the conversation's first attachment, which since the "No longer available" sample was added is the one its server let go, so it drew an empty viewer; it opens the portrait by its id.

Seen passing again on 2026-11-18: the whole of `smoke.sh` ran against a scratch instance on :4022, the server's picture, typing, push, signing out, the account switch, revocation and the member's view included. Found 2026-11-16 by the Preferences work's smoke, against a scratch instance from the tree on :4011). In `apps/android/scripts/smoke.sh`'s Server settings section, after `lumia-cli server-update --icon` the open screen shows Remove, so the change arrived, but "the screen does not draw the server's picture"; the instance stored it and serves `/api/v1/avatars/<hash>` with 200, and its log has no request for it from the app, so the app never fetched the new address. Not looked into further; the sections after it in that smoke (typing, push, signing out, the account switch, revocation, the member's view) did not run.

- [x] Updates on Linux (2026-11-14, Ryan: "let's implement the linux auto-updating, and also option 1", the option being a package repository of Lumia's own; architecture 13, "On Linux, as built"). The release makes an AppImage in place of the tarball, which could not update, and an `.rpm` beside the `.deb`. **The AppImage** updates itself: `crates/appcast` reads and writes Sparkle's feed format with the Mac's key (a feed it signs is byte for byte what Sparkle's `sign_update` makes, checked against a feed that tool signed), `src/platform/appimage.rs` checks `appcast-linux-<arch>.xml` once a day or when asked, offers only a release above its own version, and on Update holds the download until its length and signature check, then renames it over the AppImage; Restart starts the new file, which waits for the old process before taking the data directory. Preferences has an Updates card and the sidebar's foot a notice (`app/updates.rs`, the `updates` scene); nothing is downloaded without Update. **The `.deb` and `.rpm`** add Lumia's apt and dnf repository as they install, with its key (`packaging/linux/lumia-archive-key.asc`, RSA 4096, no expiry, made here; fingerprint `D3D5 B506 BACD 4A55 35BF  C490 0A19 1BD6 5B69 7D75`, replacing on the same day a first key whose only copy had been deleted before it could be kept in 1Password; no release carried the first), so a package downloaded from a release updates with the system. `scripts/linux-repo.sh` makes the signed site from a release's packages, and the release's new `packages` job deploys it to GitHub Pages once the release is published and both Linux builds succeeded. Shown in containers on arm64: the packages built on Ubuntu 22.04 with the AppImage unpacking to the same program and drawing the sign-in screen through Mesa's software driver; clippy and every new test passing on Linux, the updater's own against a feed and a download served from a local socket (a newer release offered, an old feed offering nothing, another key refused, a tampered download refused and the file untouched, a checked one put in place executable with nothing left beside it); the site built and signed on Ubuntu 24.04 with the real key; apt on Ubuntu 24.04 installing 0.9.1 from it and upgrading to 0.9.2 through the source the package added; dnf on Fedora 43 the same, importing the key at its first refresh and checking `repomd.xml`, with Fedora's rpm accepting the package signature. Found on the way: `linux-repo.sh` died in dash on an apostrophe inside `${1:?…}`, and Debian's `rpmsign` looks for `gpg2`; both fixed.
- [x] The repository's home (2026-11-14, Ryan): `packages.lumia.chat`, a CNAME to `lumia-chat.github.io`, set as the Pages site's domain with GitHub Actions as its source and HTTPS enforced; the packages are built to add `https://packages.lumia.chat` (`LUMIA_REPO_URL`'s default). The `LINUX_REPO_KEY` secret is set from the key made here, and Ryan keeps the secret key in 1Password.
- [x] The site's first deploy (2026-11-14, with `v0.10.0`). The `packages` job was refused at first, before a step ran: the `github-pages` environment Pages made allowed only `master`, and the job runs from the tag ("Tag "v0.10.0" is not allowed to deploy to github-pages due to environment protection rules"). Ryan added a tag rule `v*` beside it (the environment's rules are his; changing them was refused to Claude), and the job, re-run alone, built and signed the site and deployed it. Checked against the live site: every file served over HTTPS; apt on Ubuntu 24.04 installed `lumia_0.10.0_arm64.deb` from it and reads the repository the package added; dnf on Fedora 43 installed the rpm from it, imported the key at its first refresh and took the signed metadata.
- [x] The tarball back beside the AppImage, `.deb` and `.rpm` (2026-11-15, Ryan: "can we still release the tarball files as well?"): `Lumia-<version>-linux-<arch>-preview.tar.gz`, the program, its launcher entry, the icon, the licence and `packaging/linux/README.txt` (how to run and install it by hand, and that it does not update: the repository and the AppImage do). Made by `dist-linux.sh` in an ubuntu:22.04 container on arm64; the release uploads it with the rest of the script's output.
- [ ] Flatpak, a later channel beside the others (2026-11-15, Ryan: "keep flatpak as a future option"; architecture 13 turned it down as the only channel, not as one more). Two ways: Flathub, where its users look (built from source by Flathub from a manifest in its own repository, app id `chat.lumia.Lumia` checked against `lumia.chat`, updates by Flatpak so the shell's own updater stays off, the submission and each release's update Ryan's, on a repository not ours; its review expects an app someone has used, so after #19), or a Flatpak repository of our own on `packages.lumia.chat`, signed with the repository key. Either way the sandbox keeps its data apart from a deb's, under `~/.var/app`, and the Rune and core git dependencies are vendored for an offline build.
- [ ] Linux updates, not shown (#19): an AppImage updating itself in a real window on a Linux desktop (the check, Update and Restart are tested below the window, not through it), and the first update of a real install, which needs a release after 0.10.0; the AppImage under FUSE rather than unpacked; apt and dnf on x86-64 against the site. Shown with `v0.10.0`: both Linux jobs on runners, x86-64 included, the installed `.deb` and the AppImage each drawing the sign-in screen; the `packages` job and the live site (above); both feeds through GitHub's `latest/download` redirect, verifying with the release key. A release whose Linux job fails publishes no Linux feed, and AppImages check against a missing file until the next one.

On macOS, still to do: release notes in Sparkle's window (`generate_appcast` picks up a `Lumia-<version>.html` or `.md` beside the image; none is made), and the hand checks of Sparkle's windows listed above.
- [x] A tag builds every platform (2026-11-12, after Ryan asked for it). `release.yml` gained a `linux` job (x86-64 and arm64, on Ubuntu 22.04 for its glibc) and a `windows` job; `scripts/dist-linux.sh` makes a tarball and a `.deb` (the program, a launcher entry that takes `lumia:` links, the icon, Vulkan's loader as a dependency since the shell draws with nothing else on Linux), and `scripts/dist-windows.sh` cross-builds `Lumia.exe` with MinGW-w64 into a zip. All three assets are named `-preview` and carry a README saying so; they are waited for but do not gate the release, which is published when the Mac app and the APK are on it. Run before the jobs were written, in arm64 containers: the Linux script on Ubuntu 22.04, the `.deb` installed with apt on clean 22.04 and 24.04 with the installed program drawing the sign-in screen through Mesa's software Vulkan driver, and the Windows script to a zip whose program is a GUI one for x86-64 that asks only for libraries Windows has. **Not shown**: either job on a runner; the Linux build on x86-64 in release (debug builds and tests passed there under emulation on 2026-11-11); a window on either system; the Windows program drawing anything. Under Wine in an emulated x86-64 container it started, made its data directory and its lock, and asked Direct3D 12 for a device, at which point the emulator stopped ("rosetta error: invalid gdt selector"); that says the program loads and runs, and nothing about Windows itself.
- [x] The first release of the shell, `v0.3.0` (2026-11-12), tagged on master after all eight workflows passed there. What it showed, all for the first time on a runner: the Mac app signed with the Developer ID under the hardened runtime, notarized and stapled, as a universal binary (downloaded and checked here: Gatekeeper accepts the image and the app as "Notarized Developer ID", the appcast is signed); the iOS upload to TestFlight; the Linux tarball and `.deb` for both architectures, with the installed package drawing a frame on x86-64; the Windows zip; the instance deploy. What it did not produce is an APK, so the release stayed a draft. Two causes, one after the other: the Android release job had never run and its four signing secrets had never been set (the plan and Claude had both taken the job for proven; it was added after 0.2.1); then, with a keystore made and the secrets set, the build signed the APK and the script's check refused it, because the runner's newest apksigner (build-tools 37) words its output "V2 Signer: certificate SHA-256 digest" where the script looked for "Signer #1 certificate SHA-256". The signature was good. The script now goes by apksigner's verdict and shows its output on a failure, and `android.yml` makes the release build with a throwaway key on every change, where this would have been found. An APK needs the next tag, since a tag's jobs run the scripts of its own commit.
- [x] `v0.3.1` (2026-11-12): the first release with every job green, published as the latest. Eight assets: the Mac disk image and its appcast, the APK, the Linux tarball and `.deb` for both architectures, the Windows zip; the iOS build went to TestFlight and the instance was deployed and reports 0.3.1. The 0.3.0 draft release was deleted and its tag left. Every manifest carries the version now, the instance's included, by a "Version" commit before the tag, as 0.2.1 was done.

- [ ] The at-rest key from the platform keystore (architecture 12): the stores are in the clear, as every native shell's are. It is a core change (an encrypted SQLite natively, as the web build has) plus a keystore per platform.

Not reproduced and not looked into: whether the command waits without a limit when the instance goes away, or something else held it. Seen again 2026-11-15 by the Android agent: against a scratch instance, between the phone and a CLI account whose identity the phone's DM request had been accepted without, the command hung and was stopped.
- [ ] Core, found and not fixed: a row a device cannot read raises only a note, not an event; a wrong link code surfaces as the bare words "not found".
- [~] The flagship (`my.lumia.chat`, 2026-11-14) advertises its terms and privacy pages as `https://lumia.chat/terms` and `https://lumia.chat/privacy` (`LUMIA_TERMS_URL` and its privacy counterpart, set on the Fly app), and both answer 404 while `lumia.chat` itself answers; the web's and iOS's sign-in screens link them (the desktop shell and Android show neither). Ryan's to write and publish, or to unset until they exist. The privacy page is written (2026-11-14, `lumia-website` 68365c7, `privacy.html`, from Ryan: "update the lumia website to have a privacy policy"), checked against this document and the flagship's Fly secrets, and published the same day on Ryan's word ("push"): `https://lumia.chat/privacy` answers 200. Still to confirm, as commitments only Ryan can make or check (the operator named as Sevenshores Software Ltd. (Ryan, 2026-11-14, "for now"), `privacy@lumia.chat` as the contact, which Cloudflare Email Routing may or may not forward; when the operator reads server content; under 13; replies within 30 days; deletion by email, done by hand, until account deletion (12.1) is built). The terms page followed the same day (`lumia-website` 650bd5f, `terms.html`), published on Ryan's word: `https://lumia.chat/terms` answers 200. Its own commitments to confirm: paid plans never unlock features, 30 days' notice before a service stops, liability capped at the greater of 12 months' payments and CAD 50, the same contact address for abuse reports and appeals; it names no governing law, since the province was not known.
- [x] The browser suite's `dm` test, flaky in both browsers (2026-11-15, Ryan: "we need to fix the flakey tests"), had two causes, both product bugs. **Chromium** (`7abce28`): a query read with no data yet suspended the navigation it was read in (Solid Query reads through a resource, and TanStack Solid Router runs each navigation as a transition), so the conversation Accept opens stayed pending, and an update outside the transition read the new screen's memos as undefined and threw ("stale value from <Show>", the router's warning); the page went blank. Every query is now read through `settled()`, which answers no data while pending instead of suspending; the root route draws an error screen that says what threw and offers Reload; the suite prints every page error with its stack and can throttle Chromium's CPU (`LUMIA_E2E_SLOW`). Reproduced 5 in 6 throttled, then 54 throttled runs without it. **WebKit on the macOS runner** (`708c005`, "the CLI did not read the browser's message"): events reach a store before the DM engine reads them, and the engine read only what the gateway handed it new, so the CLI's `dm open`, ending at GroupReady with the next event stored, left it unread for good. On every welcome the engine now catches up each conversation stored past what it processed (a live test fails without it); the suite reads the CLI by one tail (`087f425`). Found with it (`d8ae56e`): the accepting device was offered the composer before its group's add-commit was acked, and a quick send was refused as pending. Together: 12 of 12 throttled Chromium and 22 loaded WebKit `link,dm` runs passed. Not yet run on the runners.
- [x] iOS `smoke-push` (2026-11-15): "no banner arrived" stopped with `483f3d5` (2026-11-07); its later failures (no link request, the gateway not connected, the tapped conversation not open) were the instance answering the phone after the core's 20 s. The app gave up on a load that failed once and asked nothing until it came to the foreground again, holding no snapshot and no loop (`8a1c580`: a failed load is asked again after 1 s, doubling to 30 s; reproduced by stalling a scratch instance 25 s at launch, then 8 of 8 passed). The macOS job ran the instance with the dev code reloader, a second and more per request on a loaded runner (`371cb62`: off, as `instance.yml` has it). Not yet run on a runner; the debug sign-in (`LUMIA_DEV_*`) still gives up after one timeout, which only the reloader change covers.
- [x] Approving a device from the apps (2026-11-15, Ryan: "I set up my private messaging on the desktop app. when I try to link my phone, there is no way to do it"). Every app could make a link request and none could approve one; only `lumia-cli identity approve`. `App::identity_link_request_summary` reads what a pasted request would add before anything is sent (`2bd12e3`); Devices gains Approve a device on the desktop (`b0ffda2`, a live test links a second computer through it), iOS (`94c55b9`, behind the phone's unlock; `smoke-approve.sh` linked a CLI through it in the simulator) and Android (`e6f4c8f`, behind the phone's unlock, the approval held across rotation; linked a CLI on the emulator); the new device's words name it on every client; the core claims a code however it was typed (`0a392c7`). Not run: the unlock prompts themselves, a browser's request approved live, TalkBack and VoiceOver; the desktop asks for no unlock (the shell cannot). Found with it: Devices names a device by the first eight characters of its id, which devices added the same day share (UUIDv7); Android's restore field may take focus only on its first line, as the approve field did.
- [x] Linking by prompt (Ryan, 2026-11-15: "there must be an easier way to link devices", then "yes go ahead with it"; identity spec 4.2.1): the new device asks, every device of the account that holds the identity prompts, the person checks the same six digits on both screens and taps once; nothing is copied or typed. A commitment to the new device's secret, revealed only after the other device answers, keeps six digits safe against an instance swapping keys. The copied request and typed code stay as "Link another way".
  - [x] Instance (`83f8370`): link sessions relayed step by step, each once, in order, from the right session; ten minutes; five open per account; `LinkSessionUpdated` on the account's log.
  - [x] Core (`0c0d171`): `link_start`, `link_progress`, `link_cancel` for the new device; `link_prompts`, `link_prompt_answer`, `link_prompt_confirm`, `link_prompt_decline` for the device that holds the identity; on UniFFI and (the new device's half) the wasm. A live test links a phone this way with matching digits.
  - [x] Clients, each by an agent from one brief, reviewed and cherry-picked: iOS (`48a9ea2`), the web as the new device only (`840e95e`), the desktop (`ec20858`: a card over the window; a live test links a second computer and turns a third away with Not me) and Android (`96d0cc4`: a sheet nothing but its buttons puts away; linked live both ways against a Rust driver on the emulator, two requests shown oldest first). "Link another way" keeps the copied request on each. The boards were drawn after (PhoneLinkWaiting and its neighbours on the canvas). Not run: the device unlock before "They match" (the emulator has none, the desktop asks for none), a `refused` request, a browser's request prompting live, any pairing across two different clients, TalkBack and VoiceOver. A device not yet signed in still links only the other way (a session needs the account). The desktop names itself "desktop" in the prompt, not the computer's name. Found with it: `has_identity` answered yes while the identity lock was busy, refusing `link_start` (`5580587`: the link calls wait for the lock).
- [x] Restoring without losing the devices still held (2026-11-15, Ryan: a friend "linked his device by using the recovery codes but it disabled his original device"). A recovery revoked every device, identity spec 4.3 step 3's default; the step also lets the person keep specific ones and nothing offered it. Core (`0ad35ab`): `identity_recover_devices` reads the roster for the phrase before anything changes, `identity_recover_keeping` keeps the devices named and revokes the rest; a live test keeps a phone through a restore on a third device. Boards drawn (PhoneRestoreAsk, PhoneRestoreKeep). Every client now asks "Do you still have a device with your private messages?" before the phrase ("Yes, link from it" goes where Link does) and, when the phrase's identity has devices, lists them unticked under "Keep any devices?" with "Restore and keep N devices" or "Restore and stop them all"; the phrase is held in memory between the two steps only. Web `4cb3909` (the flagship origin only; run live with the origin pointed at vite), iOS `ef8aae5` (the simulator smoke restores keeping the CLI's device), desktop `06e3e54` (a live test keeps the first computer), Android `06179a5` (on the emulator, keeping one of two and surviving a turn). Not run: "Yes, link from it" through to an approval, a web device's row outside unit tests, TalkBack and VoiceOver. The desktop and Android show every native device with one icon (they do not guess a phone from its label).
- [x] A revoked device erases what it kept (2026-11-15, Ryan: "for devices being revoked, can we nuke the data on it?"). Protocol 6.8 and identity spec 4.4 asked it; nothing did it, and the instance could not have said so: revoking a device ended its sessions as a sign-out does, answering `SESSION_REVOKED`. Instance (`6e9e350`): a session remembers that its device was revoked, its socket is closed with `DISCONNECT_DEVICE_REVOKED` and its calls answer `DEVICE_REVOKED`. Core (`5580587`): on either, the app erases the instance store (secure delete, then truncated and vacuumed), the identity store and keys, the reader and the tokens; a live test revokes a linked phone from the laptop and finds it holding nothing. The limit, said plainly: a device erases only when it hears; one kept offline erases when it next connects, and one never connected again keeps its data. The loop's `Closed` now carries `removed`, sent after the erase (`5d90fab`), and an erased device takes a new id, so it signs in again as a new device rather than as the revoked one until the app is reopened (`29558cf`; the live test links the removed phone again). Every client says so on the sign-in screen ("This phone was removed from your account, and its private messages were erased from it.") and forgets what the shell itself kept for the account: web `4cb3909`, iOS `ef8aae5`, desktop `06e3e54`, Android `06179a5` (each live against a scratch instance; iOS's in a kit test, not the app). A call that hears `DEVICE_REVOKED` before the loop does is remembered, so the device erases when the loop ends for any reason or, with no loop, before it signs in again (`3f23c8c`, a live test). Found with it: starting over had told the old identity's devices `DEVICE_REVOKED` and they erased; it signs them out instead (`a596495`, spec 4.7 says so now). Not done: a device that hears it by a call (not the loop) is told only that it was signed out, not that it was removed; `SESSION_REVOKED` still leaves the instance store, which protocol 6.8 also asks to wipe.
- [x] An unread count in a personal conversation that never went (2026-11-15, Ryan: "my friend says this (1) alert doesn't go away", the desktop showing one unread on a conversation whose only message was their own). The instance counts every encrypted application message of the others as unread, since it cannot read them, and a reaction, an edit, a delete or a message the device cannot decrypt is one; the shells mark a conversation read through the newest message on screen, which stops short of them, so nothing could take the count away. The core now marks a personal conversation read on through what follows that shows no message, up to the next one that does (`a86338e`), for every client at once; a live test has the other side react and fails without it (1 unread left). Not checked: which of those the friend's conversation held; the fix reaches them with the next release.
- [x] Review of linking by prompt, restoring and erasing (2026-11-15, Ryan: "verify these are real issues … and then fix all verified issues", then a second reviewer's five, of which four were the same). Fixed: the approving device read the commitment, reveal and request from the session again at every step, and the new device the answer, so an instance could choose one after seeing the other and try a million until the digits matched (both now keep what they saw, identity spec 4.2.1 amended to say so); a declined session still took the reveal and the code and could still be approved; a lost reply to an answer, a reveal or a code stalled the link or made a second approval fail; starting again, or giving up while one opened, left the first session prompting; a restore, or a revocation, that stopped a device which had revoked another produced a roster no one accepts (3.3 step 5), now re-signed (spec 4.3, 4.4); the erase check ran before only some sign-ins, a late `DEVICE_REVOKED` from the old session could erase the new device, a revocation heard by a call was forgotten at a relaunch, a retired refresh token of a revoked device answered `SESSION_REVOKED`, and a sign-in under a revoked id got a session (the instance refuses it now, 4.4); the store was vacuumed twice and the DM engine could write after the erase; a removed shell could hear `Closed` twice or before the events preceding it; the desktop read the refusal's `reason` where the instance sends `state`, and woke every frame while a poll was out; Android said "answered first" for every `FAILED_PRECONDITION`; every account event fetched the link sessions on every device (`AppEvent::LinkSessionUpdated` now); the web left a failed session open; a request wrapped across lines was refused on the desktop; the open limit raced; a long code was a 500. Instance `8a45a9b`, core `583d02a`, `b4df687`, `24021b8`, desktop `545ee5b`, web `3492430`, iOS `077e7a6`, Android `15606a1`. Checked: unit tests on every client, the core's live suite (24, with new tests for a restore after a device-signed revocation, a link started again and declined after the answer, a revoked id's sign-in, and a relaunch after hearing it), the web's link and removed steps in Chromium and WebKit, iOS's link prompt smoke, the desktop smoke (294), Android's smoke (no link step in it). Not run: any of the attacks themselves (no instance that swaps values exists to run them against; the fixes are read against the spec and the honest path is tested); Android's link prompt on the emulator; the desktop's unlock before "They match", which it still cannot ask for (spec 4.2.1 now says so). Left as it was: the new `device_revoked` column keeps its own migration, since `20261115130000` is pushed and the instance's rule allows one. A third pass by the second reviewer found two more, both real and fixed in `0a1b53d`: an approval whose bundle's reply was lost could not be tried again (the device was certified twice, `duplicate_device`; a device the roster holds with the request's key now gets only a new bundle, a live test approves one request twice), and an erase that failed on one store still erased the marker that it was to erase (the marker now goes last, only once everything else went; a unit test holds the store's write lock through an erase and finds it finished at the next launch). The relaunch after a failed erase while the other writer still holds the store is not covered: opening then fails after SQLite's ten-second wait.
- [ ] Found with the flakes (2026-11-15): Android's session very likely has the same one-failed-load gap as iOS had (not checked); the web's `Timeline` rebuilds every row when its data changes, so a message being edited may lose what was typed (one `dm` edit failure fits it); Chromium `long` failed once ("a folded body is 720 px, not ten lines of 24"); the macOS WebKit one-offs in `replies`, `editing`, `preferences` and `rail` are not explained, and will now print what threw; `apps/core/scripts/dm-faults.sh` fails its "commits: 2 refusals" case against a scratch instance, with or without these changes; the new web error screen has not been looked at.
- [ ] Instance, found and not fixed: a repeated DM request returns the pending one, so the shells' "You already asked" is never shown; an application message at a past epoch is accepted.
- [x] Protocol 14 and 16: the body grammar the shell's parser had to decide (architecture 20), for every client: the core's `lumia_core::body` since #18 (2026-11-14), and protocol 14 says what it decides.
- [ ] Not built in the shell: starting a group conversation; the avatar; unread state (no client has it and the protocol has no read state).
- [ ] Windows and Linux for real. The preview packages exist; what would let them drop the word: someone opening each on its system (a window, the section 17 rows, the hand checks with Narrator and Orca and with input methods on Wayland and X11); on Windows an installer that registers `lumia:`, a signature, an icon in the program and a build with Microsoft's toolchain if MinGW's shows a difference; on Linux following a `lumia:` link through the launcher entry; on both, notifications (Rune posts none on Linux; Windows needs an identity registered), the frame kept in settings, a tray icon or quitting with the window, and the updaters above.
- [ ] Upstream, to file or work around: Rune's font fallback does not choose a face by language (a Japanese full stop draws centred on macOS); one or two frames in a hundred begin a refresh late while scrolling; a copy across unbuilt rows is cut at 4 MiB without notice (its issue 550); `rune::single_instance` does nothing on macOS (the shell has its own lock and socket). Since `74a3c8d4`: Rune's CoreText fallback reads SFNS.ttf's names (about 55 ms) for every character the app's fonts lack and the system font has, which made the bench's first frame 3.46 s and a row with a new such character a 58 ms frame (cause and a tested patch in the entry on wide-gamut colours above); filed with the patch as Rune issue 916; bump once it lands.

## Step 10: attachments and link cards

Settled in a grill with Ryan on 2026-11-15 and written as [specs/media.md](specs/media.md), with architecture 6 ("Link cards"), 12 ("Attachments") and 20 and the deployment spec amended first. The sharer's client strips, resizes and encrypts every attachment in the core and uploads it straight to the blob store through presigned URLs; the instance builds server cards, the sharer's client builds personal DM cards. Order: the instance, the core, the desktop and the web in the main checkout, then iOS and Android by an agent each in a worktree.

- [x] The spec and the amendments (2026-11-15).
- [x] Protos and protocol (2026-11-15): `BlobRef`, the new `Attachment` and `Card`, `SendMessage.attachments` and `card_urls`, `EditMessage.remove_attachment_ids` and `cards`, `MessageCardsSet`, `SendEncrypted.blob_ids`, `DmAttachment` and `DmCard`, uploads with roles, `complete`, `GET` and `DELETE /blobs/{id}`, `POST /unfurl`, `GET /cards/{address}`.
- [x] Instance (2026-11-15). `Lumia.Uploads`: a slot per blob by role and exact length (`POST /uploads`, the two caps from `LUMIA_UPLOAD_*`, `ATTACH_FILES`), a presigned S3 `PUT` with `content-length` signed (`Req.Utils.aws_sigv4_url`) or the instance's own `PUT /blobs/upload/{token}` for a directory store, `complete` checking the stored length, `GET /blobs/{id}` answering a presigned `GET` or the bytes to whoever can read the message naming it, `DELETE` by the uploader, attachments checked on send (the sender's stored unnamed blobs, the role each place needs, the length), dropped by an edit (`remove_attachment_ids`, then no longer served), kept with the message and in its event, a personal message's `blob_ids` marked; `SweepMedia` hourly removes blobs unnamed for a day and those of messages deleted 30 days ago, and retention's purge takes a message's blobs with it. `Lumia.Cards`: `POST /unfurl` (30 fetches a minute per account, never for a personal conversation), the cache in `link_cards` (an hour, ten minutes for none), cards on a message from the cache at send or by `Workers.Unfurl` after the commit as `MessageCardsSet`, edits keeping, dropping and adding cards, the picture made by the NIF (`Lumia.Media.card`, at most 400 px, shape kept) and served by address at `GET /cards/{address}`; the fetcher on Mint connects to the address it resolved and vetted (`Lumia.Cards.Address`), TLS checked against the name, with the spec's limits. `GET /instance` names the caps. Checked by 16 new tests (uploads end to end on a directory store, caps, lengths, who may name and fetch what, edits, deletes and sweeps, a personal conversation's blobs, the presigned URL's shape; cards from the cache and after the commit, edits, the sweep, the fetcher's refusals against a stand-in for pages) and the NIF's new one. **Not verified**: a presigned upload or download against a real bucket (R2's acceptance of a signed `content-length` is assumed from S3's rules); the fetcher against the internet (tests only reach its refusals). **Not built**: account erasure taking its uploads (there is no erasure yet); a personal conversation gone for everyone taking its blobs; search of attachment names; moderators seeing a deleted message's attachments within the window.
- [x] Core, the pure parts and the fetcher (2026-11-15, by two agents in worktrees, cherry-picked). `lumia_core::media`: processing as spec 3 says (the display picture fitted under the cap, thumbnail, ThumbHash; GIF and MP4/MOV metadata wiped in place; lossless stripping for pictures sent as files, JPEG keeping APP14 as well as ICC since dropping it can change CMYK pixels; HEIC items zeroed only when stored plainly in the file), `seal`/`open` (XChaCha20-Poly1305, BLAKE3 of the plaintext), `probe` and the decode bounds; 31 tests on synthetic fixtures, the 4000×3000 fit run in release only. About 970 KB more wasm once exported. `lumia_core::cards`: `card_links` (the body parser now records `<…>` links), `parse_head`, `address_allowed`, `host_for_display`; `lumia_client::cards::fetch_card` on native, vetting every hop and pinning the resolved address; shared vectors in `proto/vectors/cards` that the instance's ports now pass too. **Not verified**: no real camera JPEG, iPhone HEIC or MOV has been through it; nothing in a browser or on a device; the fetcher has met only a local server.
- [x] Core: attachments and cards in the store and the DM projection, `App` (attach, send, edit, previews, fetch with a cache), the FFI and wasm, the CLI and a live test (2026-11-15, by an agent in a worktree). The store keeps each message's attachments (keys included) and cards (`messages.attachments` and `cards`, length-delimited protos), from `MessageCreated`, `MessageEdited` (both in full), `MessageCardsSet` (cards), dropped by a delete or a purge; `MATERIALIZED` is 2, so a store from before fetches its server channels again. The DM projection applies `DmSend.attachments` (at most 10) and `cards`, `DmEdit.remove_attachment_ids` and `cards`, and draws a card only under a link its text holds (`projection::cards_in`, through `card_links`). `lumia_client::attachments` seals and uploads (slot, `PUT` with the slot's headers and never the session, `complete`, the slot taken back on a failure), fetches and opens (`GET /blobs/{id}` on a client that does not follow redirects, the bucket asked without the session; on the web `fetch` follows), and keeps what it opened (`BlobCache`: 32 MiB in memory, 64 on the web, and natively a directory of up to 512 MiB, least recently used out first, each read checked against the asking message's hash). `App`: `upload_limits`, `attach` (process, seal, upload; the draft kept in memory by its attachment id), `set_attachment_alt`, `discard_attachment`, `send_message_with_media`, `edit_message_with_media`, `dm_send_with_media` (with `SendEncrypted.blob_ids`), `dm_edit_with_media` (new card pictures named, the dropped attachments' and cards' blobs deleted after), `dm_delete` deleting its message's blobs, `card_links`, `card_preview` (`POST /unfurl` in a server channel; natively in a personal one the core fetches the page, makes the picture a thumbnail, uploads it and keeps the card for the next send; none on the web), `attachment_bytes`, `card_image`, `attachment_drawable` (`probe` within the bounds), `placeholder_image` (ThumbHash to RGBA), `set_media_cache_dir` (native; the FFI sets `<data dir>/<host>/media` by default). `MessageSummary` and `DmMessageSummary` carry `AttachmentSummary` and `CardSummary` (`host` from `host_for_display`; bytes as base64 in the web's JSON); a notification of a message that is only attachments says "Sent a photo". The FFI (records with defaults on the new fields, so existing Swift and Kotlin initialisers stand) and the worker (`bridge.ts` typed) expose the same; `lumia-cli send --attach <file> [--as-file]` and `lumia-cli attachments <channel> <message> --out <dir>`. **Checked**: workspace tests (new: the store, the projection, the cache, the placeholder, the card rule, the notification text), the wasm check, the web's `tsc`, the desktop's clippy; against a scratch instance on a directory store the whole live suite (29) with two new tests (a photo with its camera's EXIF sent in a server channel, opened by another member upright, stripped and cached, refused under a wrong key, and no longer served once deleted; a picture sent as a file in a personal conversation, opened by the other side, its blob gone after the sender's delete), and the CLI by hand both ways. **Not verified**: a bucket (the redirect without the session has met no real presigned URL); the web worker in a browser (only type-checked); a personal conversation's card (the fetcher is not pointed at the internet from tests, and the instance refuses loopback for server cards, so no card was made end to end); Swift and Kotlin bindings built in their apps (generated once into a scratch directory to see the defaults); `Accept-Language` for a DM card is sent empty, as the core has no locale. **Left for the shells**: a platform cache directory (iOS backs up the default one under the data directory; `set_media_cache_dir` moves it), the desktop calling `set_media_cache_dir` at all (it keeps opened blobs in memory until it does).
- [x] Review of `v0.12.1..HEAD` (2026-11-15, Ryan: "verify these are real issues … and then fix all verified issues", two reviewers' lists merged). Instance, fixed with tests: the gateway crashed its session on any `{:invalid_argument, field}` (the new attachment and card refusals), and the client's outbox resent the command into the same crash; the head parser lowercased the rest of the page at every closing tag, comment or declaration (64 KiB of `</a>` took 2.8 s, a 1 MiB page minutes; now about 150 ms); the Elixir vectors test skipped every case whose card is nil; a copy of a committed send with attachments was refused as naming another message's blobs instead of meeting its own message; the Unfurl job locked the message before the channel, the opposite of edits and deletes; a link with a letter outside ASCII or a `|` was refused (`Fetch.parse` encodes it now, as the core does); fetches under way did not count against the 30 a minute, and a sent message's fetches counted against nothing (`link_cards.pending`, reserved under an advisory lock on the account, and the author's budget); `Workers.Unfurl` shared the default queue (its own, `cards: 3`) and fetched a message's three pages one after another; the card sweep could not use its GIN index (checked with `EXPLAIN`); a card made while two pictures were already in the NIF was kept without its picture for an hour; DNS was outside the 5 s, and a page and its picture had 5 s each (one deadline now, spec 6.3); a 1xx answer was taken for the final one; reading stopped at a `</head` inside a comment or a script (the tokenizer decides now, `Head.ended?`, run only when one appears) and the check re-read the whole body at every packet; an orphan named between the sweep's select and its delete was removed anyway; a removal whose object the store refused lost its row and so its retry (`uploads.deleting`, asked again hourly; card pictures likewise stay until the store lets them go); a deleted message's cards and their pictures were held for ever; deleting a message left its `MessageEdited` and `MessageCardsSet` payloads, text, attachment keys and cards, readable by any member who synced (`messages.revision_seqs`; delivery spec 10 amended); an attachment's placeholder and media type had no limit, and fields the instance does not know rode along unchecked (stripped); an empty file, 16 bytes sealed, could not be uploaded; attachments sat at the bucket's root, not `att/`; a blob's redirect was cached for five minutes to a URL good for ten after a delete (no-store, 60 s); the address rule's IPv4-translated prefix was `0:0:0:ffff::/96` instead of `::ffff:0:0:0/96`, in both ports (two vectors added); `content_address` was a dead JSON rule. And architecture hard rule 3: the instance fetched members' links with no operator's say; `LUMIA_LINK_CARDS=true` now turns cards on, off by default, `InstanceInfo.link_cards_enabled` says which, and off `POST /unfurl` answers no card, so no client needed a change (deployment spec, media spec 6.4 and architecture 6 amended). Not changed, as it cannot practically happen: a Mint connection left open by an exception after `connect` (nothing there raises). Migration `20261115160000`. Core and NIF, by an agent in a worktree, each fix with a test that fails without it: decoding skipped the allocation limit's count of the decoded pixels (`into_decoder` and `from_decoder` never ask; a 16-bit RGBA picture inside the pixel bounds needed 312 MB against 192 MiB), in the core and in the NIF's avatars and card pictures; an embedded colour profile was dropped without converting, so a Display P3 or Adobe RGB picture lost its colours (now converted to sRGB with `moxcms`, pure Rust and already in the tree under `image`, profile and lookup tables capped), in both; an attachment other than an image could go with an empty name, which the instance refuses (a name from the format now); a movie with a broken box, or cut short with its `moov` last, went as a file with its `udta` and `meta` unwiped (what follows a fault is still searched and freed, a box running past the end is read to the end, nesting past 8 is broken); a version-1 `mvhd`'s unknown duration was read as 49 days; the client's fetcher gave the page and its picture 5 s each (one deadline now); it stopped at a `</head` in a comment or script (`lumia_core::cards::head_ended`, asked only when one arrives, at most 16 times a page); and it built a TLS configuration per hop, which on Linux re-reads the system's certificates (built once now, the per-hop address pinning unchanged). Instance `175a50d`, core `d55d059`, `2d81165`, `ceace87`, NIF `b0096ee`. **Not verified**: the deadlock itself (two transactions racing were not run; the lock order is read from the code); a store that refuses a delete (the retry is tested by marking a row, not by a failing store); the colour conversion on a real phone's picture (only `moxcms`'s own Display P3 profile); the shared TLS configuration anywhere but macOS (a temporary live test fetched two real sites and refused an expired certificate there). Found on the way: LuLu, on this Mac, holds up the first connection of a freshly built test binary under `apps/core/target` by one to several seconds (a copy of the same binary elsewhere does not wait), which made the core's loopback card tests time out in bursts; the deadline test now makes a first connection before the one it times, and the other card tests have 5 s, which a burst can still exceed.

The whole browser suite passed in both browsers against that instance, its personal DM step now sending and editing through `dmSendWithMedia` and `dmEditWithMedia`. **Not verified**: a personal conversation's attachments in a browser (no file was sent or taken off there); HEIC in Safari (WebKit headless on this Mac was not given one); a video the browser cannot decode (MOV with HEVC in Chromium goes without a poster, as intended, not seen); Save in the viewer and a file's download landing on disk; a card's picture (example.com has none; the instance refuses loopback pages); keyboard and screen reader use of the strip, the tiles and the viewer; a phone-sized window; a bucket's CORS (Ryan's item below). **Known and left**: the timeline rebuilds its rows when its data changes (step 9's flakes entry), so a video or audio playing stops, and a GIF pressed to play under reduced motion is still again, when any message arrives in the conversation; the URLs survive it, the players do not. `attachmentDrawable` takes the bytes back across to the worker after `attachmentBytes` brought them out, two copies of a GIF of up to 10 MB; a call that answers the bytes only when drawable would halve it. The smokes use an http instance and never saw it. Fixed (Ryan's choice over shipping webpki roots): `lumia-ffi` exports a JNI `Tls.initialize` that sets the verifier up with the application Context, called first in `LumiaApp.onCreate`; the verifier's Android library comes from its makers' Maven repository at the version `Cargo.lock` names; an R8 keep rule. ## Step 11: roles and titles on the clients

Settled in a grill with Ryan on 2026-11-18, and written first as architecture 7.5 (with 6.7 and 7.1), authorization 3.5 and 6.4 (with 2, 3.3, 6.2, 9, 10 and 11) and protocol 7 and 9. The instance had the model, the resolution and most of the endpoints since step 2; no client knew a role existed. This step lets a server's owner, or whoever holds `MANAGE_ROLES`, create, rename, colour, reorder and delete roles, set their permissions and give them to members, from every client; shows a tag beside a name, a role or a title as the server chooses; and adds titles. Order: the documents, the instance, the core, the boards, then the desktop and the web in the main checkout, then iOS and Android by an agent each in a worktree. One release, and nothing pushed or tagged without Ryan's say.

What was decided, in short (the documents have it whole):

- **Order.** The instance's: a new role goes last above `@everyone`, a reorder is one request with the whole order, no client sends a position.
- **A role.** A name of 1 to 32 characters, its own in the server whatever its capitals; one of the nine tones or no colour; a "Show as tag" switch, on unless turned off. A new server has `@everyone` alone, shown as "Everyone", last, its permissions changeable and nothing else.
- **A title.** Free text on a membership, up to 32 characters, the member's own to set; `MANAGE_MEMBERS` changes or clears the title of someone below.
- **The tag.** The server chooses roles (the default) or titles; no falling back; the profile card shows both; the owner has no tag of its own and is marked on the card and in Members; a title's tag is the plain chip.
- **Guests** have a title and are offered no roles.
- **Permissions offered**, fourteen, the list held in the core. Server: Administrator (asks first), Manage server, Manage roles, Manage channels, Manage members, Create invites. Channels: See channels, Read message history. Messages: Send messages, Attach files, Add reactions, Mention everyone, Manage messages, Start direct messages. Not shown and kept as they were on a save: Send in threads, Start calls, Manage webhooks, Manage bots.
- **Settings** gain sections: Overview (what there is, and the roles-or-titles switch), Roles (the list in order, and an editor for each: name, colour, tag, permissions, who holds it), Members (search, each member's roles and title, both changed there, and Kick). It opens for anyone holding Manage server, Manage roles or Manage members and shows the sections they may use. The profile card shows a member's roles and title, changed there by those who may.
- **Smaller things, Claude's:** you give or take any role below your own highest, whoever the member is (the spec's rule as it was); deleting a role asks first and says how many hold it; the phones reorder with Move up and Move down.

- [x] The amendments (2026-11-18).
- [x] Protos (2026-11-18): `Role.hide_tag`, and `Role.position` an answer only; `Member.title`; `Server.member_tag` (`MemberTag`) and `UpdateServerRequest.member_tag`; `UpdateMembershipRequest.title`; `UpdateMemberRequest.roles` (`RoleIds`) and `title`, each left alone when left out, in place of its bare `role_ids`; `ReorderRolesRequest`; `AccountSnapshot.roles`, `MembershipAdded.roles`; the account events `RolesUpdated` and `MembersUpdated`.
- [x] The instance (2026-11-18). A role is created last above `@everyone` and an update never moves it; `PUT /servers/{id}/role-order` (`Servers.reorder_roles/3`) takes the whole order, holds everything at or above the actor's highest in place, and renumbers from 1 in one statement under a deferrable unique constraint on `(server_id, position)`; a server's role changes run one at a time (its row is locked). Names (1 to 32, one line, unique whatever the capitals by an index on `lower(name)`, never `everyone`), colours (the nine tones or none) and `hide_tag`; of `@everyone` only the permissions change, and it cannot be given to a member. Titles through `PATCH .../membership` (one's own) and `PATCH .../members/{id}` (someone the actor could kick), which takes roles, a title or both and does both or neither. `member_tag` among the server's settings, audited as they are. `Lumia.Workers.RolesUpdated` and `MembersUpdated` (with `member_announcements`) tell every member through `Lumia.Servers.Fanout`, which `ServerUpdated` now uses too. Roles in the account snapshot (one query) and in `MembershipAdded`. Audit rows `role.reorder` and `member.title`. The migration brings what is kept to the rules (positions numbered, names trimmed and a doubled one numbered, a colour that is no tone let go): run up and down on a copy of the dev database (420 roles, all `@everyone`), and up on a copy with five untidy roles put in by hand. Authorization 11 gained cases 11 to 16, each tagged. Checked by `mix precommit` (488 tests, 40 of them new). Found while planning and fixed here, each with a test:
  - A role's position was whatever the request said, unchecked on create (0, the top, when left out) and on update, so someone with `MANAGE_ROLES` could place a role above their own and two roles could share a place. They could not grant a permission they lacked, so it broke the order and nothing more. In every release so far.
  - A role's name and colour were not checked at all.
  - `GET /servers/{id}/members` never read `query`, which the core sends: it now keeps those whose handle or display name begins with it.
  - Replacing an overwrite on a channel already at its 50 was refused, because the limit counted the row being replaced.
  - `@everyone` could be given to a member as a role of their own (it passed the hierarchy check, being last).
  - Not seen: any of it against a running instance or from a client (tests only); the two jobs on a real queue and on two nodes; a server of thousands, where one burst of changes is a log row for every member.
- [x] The core (2026-11-18). `lumia-client/src/roles.rs`, values in and out: a server's roles in order with `Everyone` last and so named, what this account may do to each role and to each member (the instance's rules, read the same way), the fourteen permissions offered under their headings with a line each, a save that keeps what no editor offers, and the tag (a role in its tone or a title in none, no falling back). A server in the snapshot now carries its roles, `tag_titles`, `can_manage_roles`, `can_manage_members`, its owner, what this account may grant, and this account's own roles and title; a member carries its roles, title, guest and owner marks, its tag, and whether this account manages it. The calls: `create_role`, `update_role`, `delete_role`, `reorder_roles`, `set_member_roles`, `set_member_title` (one's own by the membership, another's by the member), `remove_member`, `set_tag_titles`, `role_permissions`, and `kept_members` on the bindings, which only the desktop could read before. `AppEvent::TagsChanged { server_id }` after `RolesUpdated`, `MembersUpdated`, this account's own `MembershipUpdated`, or a server changing which of the two it shows: what is kept (the snapshot's roles, the member lists) has the change before it is said, so a tag is right without asking, and a shell reads the members again. Both bindings carry all of it. Checked by fmt, clippy, 281 tests (15 of them new, beside the live one), the wasm32 check, the web's `bun run check` on the new wasm, and **live**: `roles_and_titles_reach_every_member` in `tests/live_app.rs` drives two devices against a scratch instance from the tree (roles made, refused, reordered; a role given and both devices tagging the member from their account logs; a title; the server showing titles; a moderator held to what is below it; a role deleted; a kick), run three times, and the other 29 live tests beside it. The scratch instance is down and its database dropped.
  - Not as the plan first said: the core keeps no roles table. It keeps the snapshot it last took and the member lists, as it did, and writes role and member changes into those; and it does not work out its own permissions again, since every shell takes the snapshot again on any account event and the snapshot's memberships carry them fresh (authorization 6.4 says so now).
  - The desktop builds against it and nothing more: its hand-made summaries take the new fields' defaults, `TagsChanged` has a place that does nothing yet, and the @ list boxes its member (a member is larger now). Its 325 tests pass.
  - **The phones' generated bindings are behind the core** until their step: `apps/apple/scripts/core.sh` and `apps/android/scripts/core.sh` will regenerate `ServerSummary`, `MemberSummary` and `AppEvent` with the new fields and the new case, and the Swift and Kotlin that build those by hand or switch over events will not compile until they are given them. The web's TypeScript types (`bridge.ts`) do not name the new fields yet either; nothing there breaks.

Every claim was checked by a failing test, a run or a measurement before it was fixed, and each was judged for whether it can happen to a real server or phone. Done by Claude and seven agents in worktrees (four of them replacements on Opus after the first ones hit a usage limit mid-way), each commit cherry-picked: `a3d2f16` the instance, protos and documents; `22ef5bf`, `76fa913`, `a26aa91`, `323938d` the shells' own findings; `ff07f01` the core; `5f102bb` a DM retry; `43ca687`, `beb08a6`, `576c8f1`, `239fcd1` the shells on the new calls; `d72eb96` the old calls removed.
  - **A save sends what was changed** (architecture 7.5, authorization 3.5, protocol 9 amended). Every editor sent the whole role, and every member save the member's whole set of roles from a list a second old, so two admins at work undid each other, a permission just revoked among what came back. The instance now takes `PATCH /servers/{id}/roles/{role_id}` (`UpdateRoleRequest`: what is named, permissions as grant and revoke) and `add_role_ids`/`remove_role_ids` on the member PATCH; the core has `change_role(was, now)` and `change_member(give, take, title)`, one request for a member's roles and title together (which ends the two-request save, roles landing while the title was refused); every shell's editor keeps the role or member as it opened beside the draft, offers Save against that and not the live one, and follows a change from elsewhere while untouched (the sheet's `followed`, `member_changes`). `update_role`, `set_member_roles` and `set_member_title` are gone from the core. Checked live on every shell: a rename saved from an editor open while another session switched a permission off leaves it off, and a role given elsewhere survives another being taken in a row opened before.
  - **No change left untold.** A role or member change could be made one with a waiting `RolesUpdated`/`MembersUpdated` job that then began before the change committed and read without it; nothing told the members until the server's next change. Seen on a scratch database: a job begun against an open change now waits for its commit and tells of it. A change holds the server's row until it commits, the jobs read under it and run one at a time per server (an advisory lock), which also closes the reorder-against-hierarchy-check race (authorization 6.4).
  - **The migration on untidy roles.** Seen failing on a scratch database with "Mod", "mod" and "Mod 2" and an `@everyone` an older release let be moved; it now puts `@everyone` last again and numbers a doubled name with a number nobody has.
  - **Names and titles are what they look like**: line separators, direction marks and characters of no width are refused (joiners allowed, for emoji). **A member's own title** changes ten times an hour in a server, since each change is a log row for every member (authorization 10).
  - **Sends.** In the core, a message, edit, delete or reaction was held in memory while the instance could not be reached and dropped with the next failed attempt, no kill needed: it is in the outbox before the call returns, its draft cleared in the same write. On Android and iOS the draft was cleared before the send was called, and the desktop's personal send cleared it on spawning: all keep the words until the send returned. The web sent a failed send's words into whichever conversation was on screen; a quick double send could collapse into one; both fixed.
  - **Members kept.** The core kept every server's members as one value (10.9 MB with two servers of 10,000; 27 to 77 ms and a full rewrite for one title): a row each now, one change 10 to 340 µs. A list read a page at a time no longer writes over what the account log said meanwhile, and after the log is taken from a snapshot every kept list is read again. The desktop read a tag and the server-DM switch from whichever server listed a person last, and asked for a server's whole list on every notice (one at a time now). Every shell said "Nobody holds this role" when the list was not known; two phones stored a failed read as an empty list.
  - **Smaller**: a store write no longer waits holding the core's lock (one draft save stalled every call: 1.7 s measured on iOS); iOS reads and keeps drafts off the main thread; Android starts the gateway before reading member lists; the viewer's drag ends when released outside it; "You" and "Owner" (or "Guest") both on one's own card; the web's editor of a role just made held empty fields; a refused DM commit is retried once the other one has merged (`dm-faults.sh` failed most runs on master, and passes every run now).
  - Left, with the reason: a new client on a v0.13.4 instance stripping roles (no released client sends that field, and there are testers only); a removed member told of one more change (nothing they could not see a moment before); the desktop's draft write on the window's thread (measured: 0.3 ms median, 22 ms at the 99th percentile under a catch-up, one 191 ms in 477; nothing holds a write near the timeout). Refuted: Android's drafts on the main thread (they were not).
  - Not seen: a real window, a device, a server of thousands, a screen reader; the phones' and the web's handling of a log taken from a snapshot was read, not run; the failing sends in the browser and on iOS are tested below the screen.
- [x] Guests see only the members they share a channel with (2026-11-19). A guest could list every member of a server through `GET /servers/{id}/members`, was told of every member's roles and title, and was told the presence of everyone watching the server, where architecture 6.2 says a guest never sees the member list. Authorization 6.4 and protocol 9 and 6.7 now say what a guest sees: itself and the members it shares a channel of the server with (a server DM among them), and no one else; the server's roles and settings reach it as they reach anyone, since a tag is drawn from the roles. The members endpoint answers a guest with those (search and pages as before); `Fanout.to_members` tells a guest a `MembersUpdated` of those alone, or none; a guest's presence, at first and in each diff, lists those alone, the set read again for each diff. Checked by `mix precommit` (507 tests, 3 new, each failing with its filter taken out) and against a scratch instance from the CLI: a guest's `members` lists itself, a member's everyone.

## Step 12: custom emoji

GitHub #17. Settled with Ryan on 2026-11-20 and written first as [specs/emoji.md](specs/emoji.md), with architecture 6.6 (and 1's spec list, 12, 15.2, 16 and 19), protocol 9, 10 and 14 and authorization 3.3, 6.2, 6.4 and 10 amended. Order, as step 11's: the documents, the protos, the instance, the core, the boards on the canvas for Ryan's look, then the four shells from the core's rules (the desktop and the web, then iOS and Android by an agent each in a worktree). Nothing pushed or tagged without Ryan's say.

What was decided, in short (the spec has it whole):

- **Ryan's**: a server's emoji are used **in that server only** (its channels and server DMs; not in other servers, not in personal conversations); a removed emoji's **picture goes and its name stays** (messages show `:name:`, reactions stay counted as `:name:`, no new ones); **animated from the start** (overruling the proposal of still pictures first); the limit is **the operator's, per server** ("so I can lock the number behind a premium plan"): `LUMIA_SERVER_EMOJI_MAX` (50) and `bin/lumia-admin emoji-limit <server> <n|default>`, lowering never deleting.
- **Claude's**, from the architecture's precedents: refs beside the text with byte offsets, as mentions are (the core finds them at send, the instance drops a ref to a removed emoji instead of refusing it); reactions by `emoji_id` beside the `reaction` string, which the instance fills with `:name:` for old clients; pictures made by the NIF within 128 px, still as PNG, animated as GIF (no Rust-only animated WebP encoder; Android's decoder plays no APNG), sealed and served by address as avatars are; `EmojiUpdated` on every member's log as `RolesUpdated` is; names of 2 to 32 of a-z, 0-9 and `_`; the picker's server section after Frequently used and before Unicode's groups; `:` in the composer listing the server's emoji; an Emoji section in Server settings.

- [x] The spec and the amendments (2026-11-20).
- [x] Protos (2026-11-20): `CustomEmoji`, `EmojiRef`, `Message.emoji`, `SendMessage.emoji`, `EditMessage.emoji`, `MessageEdited.emoji`, `emoji_id` on the reaction commands, events and counts, `Server.emoji_limit`, `MANAGE_EMOJI` (7), `EmojiUpdated` (44), `AccountSnapshot.emoji`, `MembershipAdded.emoji`, `ListEmojiResponse`, `UpdateEmojiRequest`. The core only builds against them so far (empty values where it builds these messages by hand); `GatewayCommand::Send` now boxes its `Command`, which the new field made large enough for clippy's `large_enum_variant`.
- [x] The instance (2026-11-20). Until then the desktop draws a ref as its `:name:` text, and reactions, the picker and settings, which are not inline text, are unaffected.

## Open questions carried forward

From architecture §20, plus ones raised while specifying:

- MLS ciphersuite and key-package cadence.
- OpenMLS on wasm32 with WebCrypto key storage, or an adjusted web trust statement.
- The Rune shell's proof obligations and what it needs from each platform (architecture §20; the checklist is step 9).
- Whether `commands` retention of 30 days is acceptable for non-message operations (delivery §9 states the consequence).
- The connections row of architecture §17: 112 KB of server memory per connection against the 80 KB budgeted, measured 2026-11-06 on a laptop ([spikes/2026-11-06-load.md](spikes/2026-11-06-load.md)); a Linux node's reading and the transport's heap under `fullsweep_after` are the next two things to look at.
